### New finding

| # | Sev | Item | What | Fixed? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F34 | High | "Answer only from my documents" does nothing when the chat has no attached indexed document | `apps/mobile/src/screens/Chat.tsx:389` gates the whole retrieval branch on `docs.ready`, and `useDocumentContext` (`documents/hooks.ts`) sets `ready = documents.some((d) => d.chunkCount > 0)` over `library.attachedTo(chatId)`. So when the chat has nothing attached — the normal state right after the user deletes the document, and also the state of every fresh chat — the strict branch, the `noAnswer` check and `t("documents.notFound")` are all skipped and the model answers from its own weights. The switch's own copy promises the opposite: `documents.strict.hint` = "When nothing relevant is found, Inborn says so instead of guessing." Nothing on screen contradicts it either: `chat.attach.strict` ("DOCS ONLY") renders only inside the attachment chip row (`Chat.tsx:1021`), so with nothing attached there is no strict indicator in the chat at all. Reproduced on the emulator (empty library, and non-empty library with nothing attached) and on the simulator, on Instant and on Fast; both times the answer was confidently wrong about a place that does not exist ("check the official Reykjavik Depot website", "this is classified intelligence information"). Repro: turn the switch on, open a chat with no attachment, ask anything. Evidence `a-05`, `a-06`, `a-14`, `i-10`, `i-11`. The fix is to let the strict path run whenever `docs.strict` is on, not only when `docs.ready` — with no attached document, retrieval returns nothing, which is exactly the `noAnswer` case. | **Fixed in round 16** (`fixes-r16`): `apps/mobile/src/lib/docsGate.ts` decides the turn before the model is asked — strict with nothing attached answers the new `documents.noneAttached`, strict with an unindexed attachment answers `documents.notFound`, and the DOCS ONLY tag now follows the switch rather than the attachment chips. Proof on the emulator and the iPhone 15 Pro simulator in `docs/qa/fixes-r16/`. |

### Observations (not failures)

- **O38 (harness, iOS)**: `idb ui text` types through the simulator's *active* keyboard. This simulator carried `he_IL@sw=Hebrew`
  in `AppleKeyboards`, so an English question arrived in the composer as Hebrew gibberish
  (`ם׳ צשמט ברשאקד ׳קרק בםומאקג שא איק קטלחשהןל גקפםא?`) and the model answered a nonsense question in Hebrew
  (`i-12-o38-hebrew-keyboard-garbled.png`). Fix: `xcrun simctl spawn <udid> defaults write -g AppleKeyboards -array
  "en_US@sw=QWERTY;hw=Automatic" "emoji@sw=Emoji"`, then shutdown + boot the simulator. Worth knowing before any typed-input row.
- **O39 (harness, iOS)**: the app's `Toggle` primitive ignores a zero-duration `idb ui tap`. Four taps at the correct
  centre left "Answer only from my documents" off; `idb ui tap --duration 0.15` flipped it on the first try.
- **O40 (harness, iOS)**: `idb ui describe-all` returns frames in **scroll-content** coordinates, not screen coordinates, so a
  row below the fold reports a `y` well past the 852 pt screen (the vault's `install-fast` at `y 1140`) and tapping its centre
  lands nowhere. The vault install went through `EXPO_PUBLIC_AUTOINSTALL=file` + `Documents/dev-vault.txt` (`install fast`)
  instead; that also switched the default model, so no separate "use" command was needed.
- **O41**: over the spreadsheet, **Instant** cited the right sheet but got the comparison wrong — "Which site holds the most
  crates?" answered "**Nairobi**" (117) where sheet 1 has Reykjavik at 5,842 (`a-15-xlsx-sheet-citation.png`). The vault rates
  Instant "Weak · Documents" and the disclaimer strip is on screen, so this is the documented weakness of the bundled default
  rather than a retrieval defect — but it is the shipping out-of-the-box answer quality for a numeric question over a sheet.
- **O42 (device guard)**: on the 4 GB guest the Fast card shows **"TOO BIG FOR 4 GB"** with no Install button, which is the
  guard doing its job. Forcing it with `EXPO_PUBLIC_DEV_RAM_GB=8` installs and loads Fast, but the guard then fires during the
  first document answer — `[device] memory · device.memory.switched {"model":"Instant"} · act/switchToSmaller` and
  `[inborn] engine unloaded (memory)` — and the ask returns an **empty answer** with `predicted_n 0` and `0.0 tok/s` while the
  chips still render. The banner "Ran out of memory · Switched to Instant · SWITCH BACK" is correct and nothing crashed, but the
  empty assistant turn is what the user is left with. Row 2 was therefore re-run on a 6 GB guest. Same shape as O33.
- **O43 (harness)**: `EXPO_PUBLIC_AUTOINDEX` re-imports its fixtures on **every** mount of the Documents screen (`devDone` is a
  per-mount `useRef`), so a fixture deleted in row 4 comes back as a new record the next time the library is opened. The
  post-delete checks have to be done without reopening the library; the `D16` hash dedupe is what keeps a re-import from
  doubling a document that is still there.
- **O44 (harness)**: `scripts/serve-models.mjs` takes its port from the **`PORT` environment variable**, not a `--port` flag
  (`scripts/serve-models.mjs:18`); `--port 8802` is silently ignored and the server listens on 8790. With the reverse mapped to
  8802 the app showed "Could not install: fetch failed: java.io.IOException: unexpected end of stream" and the server logged
  zero requests. The pass-6 note "serve-models on 8802" reads like a flag; it is `PORT=8802 node scripts/serve-models.mjs`.

### Process audit (end of pass 7)

Emulator `emulator-5572` killed with `adb emu kill` after the Android rows (`pgrep -fl qemu-system` empty). Simulator
`12114C34-288A-4AAB-B50A-693DC6775C60` shut down — **`xcrun simctl list devices booted` lists nothing**. All four Metro
processes (8171 ×3, 8081) and `serve-models` killed by pid; `lsof -nP -iTCP:8081 -iTCP:8171 -iTCP:8790 -sTCP:LISTEN` is empty.
No browser was opened. `adb devices` shows only `<6t-serial>` (the OnePlus 6T, another stream's phone), which was not touched;
the USB iPhone was not touched. No gradle daemon was left running (`--no-daemon`, private `GRADLE_USER_HOME` under the scratch
dir); `gradlew --stop` was never used.

### New finding (soak 6, 22.9.2026)

| # | Sev | Item | What | Fixed? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F38 | Low | Answer length on a small model | **Filed from Moshe's observation on the phone, watching soak 6 at 01:00**: "when the bot answers it really digs / rambles". The soak-6 log showed "What is 2 plus 2?" answering in 8 s the first time it was asked in a chat, then 83–87 s on every repeat, and once 139 s on a Hebrew ask — but soak 7 and section Q of `docs/qa/purchases-run-2026-09-11.md` proved those long numbers were **the measuring driver, not the model**: it read only the answer's first line, an identical repeated answer never changes that line, so the poll ran to its own ceiling every time. Underneath that driver artifact the root cause is real: nothing in a turn ever said how long its answer should be, so the default 0.8B model could elaborate as long as its 1,024-token cap allowed. | **Fixed in round 19 as a product improvement, not a hang fix** (`fixes-r19`, `packages/core/src/chat/length.ts`): one answer-length plan per turn (short 224 / chat 512 / draft-translate-code 1,024 / hands-free 160, or an explicit word/sentence count the user asked for), threaded through every generate path on every tier. Retested on the real Play path (vc13 → vc14, section Q): the same repeated arithmetic question completes in 8–17 s on vc13 itself and 3–5 s on vc14; an "in about 200 words" ask produced complete, un-truncated answers of 99 and 160 words depending on chat context, never cut mid-sentence. **Stays unverified**: which exact answer Moshe saw on the phone at 01:00 (the soak-6 log names several candidates); Hebrew on Instant remains gibberish either way — its own catalog card says "No · Hebrew", and this fix does not touch that. |
| F39 | Medium | Answer length on a small model | **Round 19 shortened the wrong questions.** MosheAI, reviewing the round-19 fix (22.9.2026 05:10): `isShortAsk` calls any one-line question up to 16 words a short factual ask, so **"How do I set up SSH keys on my Mac?" got the short plan** — 224 tokens and the line "Answer in one to three sentences". A how-to, an explanation, a comparison or a procedure is one line long but its answer is not; answered in three sentences it is not an answer. Measured before the fix on the `Pixel_6_API_33` emulator (release, Instant): 47 words of prose, no steps. | **Fixed in round 20** (`fixes-r20`): `isExplanatoryAsk` in `packages/core/src/chat/length.ts` runs ahead of the counting rule and keeps such a turn on its use's own length (`moderate`, 512 tokens). Three small tables in the eight shipped locales plus Hebrew: Latin-script question words read at the start of the line, CJK and Hebrew markers read anywhere, and a short exception list so "How much does it cost?" / "Wie viel kostet das?" stay short. Explicit length ("briefly", "in one sentence", "in about N words") still wins, a draft is still long, and a spoken turn is still spoken. Real one-liners are unchanged: "What is the capital of France?" and "What is 2 plus 2?" still plan `short` / 224. 12 tests in `packages/core/test/fixes-r20.test.ts`; before/after on the `Pixel_6_API_33` emulator and one iPhone 15 Pro simulator run in `docs/qa/fixes-r20/`. |

### New findings (real web build and real desktop build, 22.9.2026)

Both found by opening the shipping artefacts themselves, not by a test: the web export served on
`http://127.0.0.1:8477`, and the ad-hoc-signed `Inborn.app`. Round 21 detail and proof:
`docs/qa/fixes-r21-2026-09-22.md`; screenshots in `docs/qa/fixes-r21/`.

| # | Sev | Item | What | Fixed? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F40 | **High** | Web onboarding (S02) | **The web build died on the first onboarding click.** On the real export, after the download door, pressing **Continue** on S01 Welcome left a blank page: `pageerror: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'get')`. Root cause: S02 `screens/Onboarding/ModelChoice.tsx` reads `useInstalledModel` from `../../vault`, `vault/index.ts` re-exports `resolveEngine` from `vault/resolve.ts`, that imports `adapters/llamaRn.ts`, and `llama.rn` calls `TurboModuleRegistry.get("RNLlama")` at module scope — react-native-web has no `TurboModuleRegistry`, so Metro's first evaluation of that module threw. Harmless on the phones, and invisible to the gate: `pn web:smoke` set `localStorage["inborn.prefs"] = {onboarded:true}` on its context and had never opened an onboarding screen, so it **passed on the broken build**. | **Fixed in round 21** (`web-desktop-check`): `apps/mobile/src/vault/resolve.web.ts`, a Metro platform variant returning `null`, so `llama.rn` never enters the web bundle — the same discipline as `screens/vault/VaultEntry.web.tsx`. Plus `vault/store.ts`: `modelLocation()` no longer calls `devFallbackFile()` on the web, which built an `expo-file-system` path under a document directory no browser has. Gate closed two ways: `scripts/web-smoke.mjs` now walks the real S01→S05 onboarding and fails on any page error after every screen and every click (negative control run today: with the fix removed it exits 1 at `onboarding-welcome (after the click)`), and `apps/mobile/test/webBundle.test.ts` walks every route under `src/app` in Metro's web resolution order and fails if any native-only module is reachable, printing the import chain. Proof on the rebuilt export at 1280×800, headless: `docs/qa/fixes-r21/fixes-r21-02-onboarding-model.png` (S02 rendering) and `-03-chat-answer.png` (Paris, chip INSTANT). |
| F41 | Medium | Desktop shell (Tauri) | **The desktop window wore the browser's doors.** `WebShell` is mounted from `app/_layout.tsx` for every web-platform build, and the Tauri shell is one, so the desktop app showed the browser notice strip ("Runs locally in your browser… Get the app") and the **download door** — a gate asking the user to download a model the Rust vault already holds. Those doors read `web/boot.ts`, which the desktop never runs: `adapters/prepare.web.ts` routes the shell to `prepareTauri()`. | **Fixed in round 21**: `apps/mobile/src/web/doors.ts` exposes `webDoorsApply()` over the existing `isTauri()`; `WebShell` renders `BrowserShell` only when it is true and otherwise passes its children through untouched. Pinned by `apps/mobile/src/web/doors.test.ts` (browser gets the doors, a `__TAURI__` window does not, no `window` counts as a browser). **Runtime proof NOT RUN** — Moshe is at this Mac and this stream may not launch an app or put anything on his screen; `desktop:check` passes and the team lead is scheduling the launch. |
### New finding (wide windows, 22.9.2026)

| # | Sev | Item | What | Fixed? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F42 | Medium | Wide windows (web + desktop) | **Wide screens rendered the phone shell.** Moshe, 22.9.2026: *"on large screens like desktop the app must have a desktop design, not a mobile one, depending on the screen size."* The web export and the Tauri window stretched the phone layout across the whole window at any width: one column, the header's "Chats" button as the only door to the chat list, and bottom sheets rising from the bottom edge of a 1,440 px window. Spec §8.9 (sidebar 280 px with chats / projects / vault / proof / settings, message column 680 px, optional document-citation panel, Cmd/Ctrl shortcuts and a command palette) and §9.7 (window minimum 1,040×720) had described the desktop shell from the first draft; nothing in the app read the window width. | **Fixed in round 22** (`desktop-layout`): one threshold table in `apps/mobile/src/lib/layout.ts` (phone < 760, wide ≥ 760, desktop ≥ 1,040) drives a permanent sidebar built from the existing chats drawer (`components/shell/ChatsPane.tsx`), a 680 px centred message column, the document/citation panel through the `onOpen` hook `Citations` already carried, centred dialogs instead of bottom sheets, the §8.9 key map (`lib/desktopKeys.ts`, also three new Tauri menu accelerators) and a command palette. The phone shell is unreachable above the threshold only because the app is portrait-locked, so it is untouched. Found on the way and fixed: `chatCreated` never bumped `chatsVersion`, so the permanently mounted sidebar list missed a chat the moment it was created. 10 new tests (`lib/layout.test.ts`, `lib/desktopKeys.test.ts`); proof against the real web build in `docs/qa/desktop-layout/` and `docs/qa/desktop-layout-2026-09-22.md`, including a byte-identical A/B of the 390×844 phone screens against `origin/main`. The Tauri window is deliberately not photographed: launching a freshly built `Inborn.app` re-signs it ad hoc and the vault key's Keychain ACL then asks the operator for a password on screen, so the desktop shell stays with the `web-desktop-check` stream. |

### New finding (real iPhone, build 12 device pass, 22.9.2026)

Found by looking at the phone during the build-12 pass, not by a test: every screen of the shipped TestFlight 1.0.0 (12)
build carried an amber "Ran out of memory · Switched to Instant" line at launch, on a phone that had answered a chat
turn on the same 1.2 GB Fast model two hours earlier (`docs/qa/cdn-iphone-2026-09-22.md`, 13:48). Full pass and
screenshots: `docs/qa/ios-device-pass-12-2026-09-22.md`, `docs/qa/ios-device-pass-12/f43-banner-t06.png` and
`-t90.png`.

| # | Sev | Item | What | Fixed? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F43 | **High** | Device guard, iOS memory row (§6.5) | **The guard drops the chosen model on iOS because the *system* is under memory pressure, not the app.** On Moshe's iPhone 13 Pro the shipped build refused to load Fast on **every** cold launch and fell back to Instant with the line "Ran out of memory · Switched to Instant · SWITCH BACK". It is not true that the app ran out of memory: across 16 device launches Inborn's own phys footprint sat at **223 MB** and the engine never once opened the Fast file — `grep` over every launch log finds `instant.gguf` (4 loads) and never `Qwen3.5-2B-Q4_K_M.gguf`, so the switch happened **before any load was attempted**, not because a load failed. Fast itself is fine: one tap on SWITCH BACK loaded it and it answered `The capital of France is Paris.` at **13.2 tok/s**, ledger `MODEL FAST · QUANT Q4_K · FIRST TOKEN 683 ms · 138 + 6 tokens`. **Root cause, from the device log**: `apps/mobile/modules/device-guard/ios/DeviceGuardModule.swift` subscribes to `DispatchSource.makeMemoryPressureSource(eventMask: [.warning, .critical])`, which is a **system-wide** signal about the whole phone, and forwards it verbatim as this app's `memoryPressure`. `packages/core/src/device/policy.ts` then reads that as "this app is out of memory" and `tierBelow` sends a phone straight to Instant. The syslog for the controlled relaunch shows the system event arriving **4.0 s before the app even started**: `14:57:51.333 duetexpertd ATXMemoryPressureMonitor: received memory pressure warning of type: critical`, then `14:57:55.344 SpringBoard Sending launch request … com.inbornapp.mobile`. A 6 GB iPhone with a normal set of apps open is under system pressure routinely, so on that phone the guard fires on essentially every launch. **Not fully isolated**: the boot read in `apps/mobile/src/device/signals.ts` (`memoryPressure: snap ? (memoryHealthy(snap) ? "normal" : "warning") : "unknown"`, where `memoryHealthy` is `availableMemory > 150 MB` from `os_proc_available_memory()`) is a second path to the same banner, and this run could not sample `os_proc_available_memory()` on the device to rule it out. The system-wide subscription is the one the log positively shows firing. **Why the line never clears**: that part is by design, not a second bug — after `MEMORY_RECOVERY_MS` (30 s) the status becomes `memoryBack`, and `policy.ts` renders `memoryBack` with the **same** `memorySwitched` headline on purpose, "until the user takes Switch back or dismisses it". The banner was still up at t+90 s in the controlled relaunch. **Reproduction, exact**: install Fast on an iPhone (no `defaultModelId` in `Documents/models/vault.json`, so `VaultStore.activeModel()` falls through to `recommendedId()` = Fast), put the phone under normal system memory pressure, cold-launch the app, and read the chat header: it says `INSTANT` with the amber line, at t+6 s and still at t+90 s. **Cosmetic knock-ons**: on the hands-free screen the banner clips its own headline, which renders as "Voice input needs the" with the rest cut; on the paywall it takes the slot holding "No subscription. No account. Yours forever." **Suggested fix, not applied here** (this stream is a release stream and did not touch product code): gate the switch on the app's own headroom — treat the system-wide `DispatchSource` event as a hint, and only act when `os_proc_available_memory()` is genuinely near the model's resident size, or when a load actually fails. A user who chose a model should not have to re-choose it on every launch. | **Fixed in round 23** (`fixes-r23`), and the "not fully isolated" second path turned out to be the dominant one. **(1) The boot RAM floor, proven on the phone.** `apps/mobile/src/device/signals.ts` read RAM as raw GiB (`bytes / 2**30`, ~5.5 on a "6 GB" phone) while the vault, the catalog, the chip class and onboarding read `marketingRamGB` (6). Fast's `minRamGB` is exactly **6**, so `bootModel()` decided Fast did not fit, started Instant and called `noteBootSwitch()` — which renders this very line, once per cold launch, latching until SWITCH BACK. That is why the engine never opened the Fast file. The phone showed both readings at once: vault header `RUNS ON: IOS-MID · 6 GB` (that class needs >= 6) against the pass-12 ledger's `CONTEXT 144 / 2048` (`policy.ts` caps context at 2048 below 6 GB). One reading now: `ramGBFromBytes` in `packages/core/src/device/chip.ts`, called by the guard and by the vault. **(2) The system-wide subscription, fixed as filed.** iOS memory events now carry `source: "app" | "system"`, and `memoryPressureFromIos` (`packages/core/src/device/ios.ts`, the counterpart of `memoryPressureFromAndroid`) believes the process's own `UIApplication` warning always and a phone-wide `DispatchSource` event only while `os_proc_available_memory()` is at or below the 150 MB floor `memoryHealthy` already used. No threshold was loosened. **(3) The wording, decided rather than left.** §6.5 keeps this line standing until the user acts, which is right for a real eviction and wrong for the boot floor, where nothing was evicted and nothing was mapped. `SwitchReason` now carries a third value, `"fit"`, which `noteBootSwitch` uses: the row still stands until SWITCH BACK or a dismiss, because the user's own choice was overridden, but it reads "Not enough memory for the model you chose · Started on Instant" in muted tone instead of the amber "Ran out of memory". 12 tests (`packages/core/test/device-ios.test.ts` 4, the F43 block of `device-boot-tier.test.ts` 4 pinning the boot floor to `ramFit` for five real phone sizes, `device-policy.test.ts` and `mapState.test.ts` 4 on the new reason). **What was not measured**: the literal `os_proc_available_memory()` value still could not be sampled on the device — an embedded `--dev` bundle redboxes with "Cannot create devtools websocket connections in embedded environments" and the Release bundle has no console — so that path is settled indirectly: `memoryHealthy()` and its 150 MB floor are unchanged by this round, and the fixed build raised no banner on three cold launches, so the read was above 150 MB every time. The margin above the floor is still unknown. **Proved on the phone**: a Release build of the fix installed over 1.0.0 (12) with the vault intact drew no banner on three cold launches, its header read FAST, its log opened `Qwen3.5-2B-Q4_K_M.gguf` each time and `n_ctx` moved 2048 -> **4096**. **Fast answers without any tap on SWITCH BACK**: asked "What is the capital of France?" on a cold launch it returned `The capital of France is Paris.`, ledger `MODEL FAST · QUANT Q4_K · CONTEXT 144 / 4096 · 13.1 tok/s · FIRST TOKEN 680 ms · 138 + 6 tokens` (`after-08`, `after-09`). Reinstalling the shipped 1.0.0 (12) on the same phone brought the banner straight back. `docs/qa/fixes-r23/`, `README.md` section "Fixes round 23". |

### The two knock-ons of that banner, filed separately (22.9.2026)

Both were noted in the F43 row above as cosmetic; they are layout bugs of their own and are fixed in round 23.
F44 is the desktop stream's.

| # | Sev | Item | What | Fixed? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F45 | Medium | §8.8 status strip | **The guard's banner hid content on screens that do not pad for it.** The strip is drawn as an absolute overlay from `app/_layout.tsx` at `insets.top + 52`, and only Chat and Chats consumed the `BannerInsetContext` height that QA F13 introduced. On the hands-free screen it covered the second line of its own headline, which read as "Voice input needs the" with the rest cut; on the paywall it took the slot holding "No subscription. No account. Yours forever." | **Fixed in round 23** by extending that same context rather than moving the strip: `components/shell/bannerInset.tsx` now exports `<BannerSpacer />`, and every screen container reserves the measured height — `components/shell/Screen.tsx` (the 18 routes built on it, scrolling and not), `VaultScreen`, `DocumentsScreen`, `PaywallScreen`, and `HandsFreeScreen` (which pads instead of inserting a child, because its root is `justify-content: space-between`). Chat and Chats were moved onto the shared component so there is one spacer, not five. The height alone was not the answer: the strip is drawn `BANNER_TOP` = 52 below the safe area, so a screen with a smaller gap of its own still lost its first line — the first attempt at this fix moved the hands-free headline from "Voice input needs the" to "transcription model" and was caught on the phone before it was committed. `useBannerPad(ownTopGap)` reserves `BANNER_TOP + height - ownTopGap`, and `BannerSpacer` measures where it actually sits. The arithmetic is a pure module, `apps/mobile/src/lib/bannerGeometry.ts`, with 6 tests that pin exactly the case the first attempt got wrong. **Proved on the phone** with a real `ThermalSerious` condition raising the strip on four screens: hands-free shows its whole headline and the paywall its "No subscription. No account. Yours forever." `docs/qa/fixes-r23/`. |
| F46 | Low | Vault Details sheet (S31) | **Sharp's SOURCE row read `play-asset-pack, play-asset-pack, https` on an iPhone.** For a model that is not installed, `ModelDetails.tsx` printed `model.delivery.map(d => d.kind)` raw: untranslated internal kind names, a Google Play source on a device that has no Play, and the same source twice because Sharp is split into two Play packs. | **Fixed in round 23**: `deliverySources(model, os)` in `packages/core/src/catalog/manifest.ts` returns the platform's sources in manifest order with each named once — Play only on Android, Apple packs only on Apple — as `DeliverySource` values, which are the `vault.source.*` keys the sheet already uses for an installed model. Sharp on iOS now reads `models.inbornapp.com`, Instant reads `This app, models.inbornapp.com`, and Sharp on Android reads `Google Play, models.inbornapp.com`. 5 tests in `packages/core/test/catalog-manifest.test.ts`, including one that every source the shipped catalog can produce has a `vault.source.*` label. **Proved on the phone**: the XCUITest driver opened the not-installed Sharp's Details sheet and it reads `SOURCE · models.inbornapp.com`. `docs/qa/fixes-r23/after-06-details-sharp-source.png`. |
### New finding (unattended desktop QA, 22.9.2026)

| # | Sev | Item | What | Fixed? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F44 | **High** | Desktop QA (process) | **The desktop app could not be tested without Moshe.** Moshe, 22.9.2026: *"Desktop: it's not one dialog, it's a million. Find a way to test it WITHOUT me entering a password and WITHOUT you moving my mouse all the time."* Two separate walls. (a) The vault key and the license-cache key are login-Keychain items whose ACL names the code identity that created them; dev builds are signed ad hoc (`"signingIdentity": "-"`), which identifies an app only by its own code hash, so every rebuild was a stranger and macOS asked for the login password on launch. (b) There is no WebDriver for WKWebView on macOS — `tauri-driver` is Linux/Windows only — so driving the window meant a human pointer. The cost is in the record: F41 and F42 both shipped with *runtime proof NOT RUN*, and the round-22 desktop layout was signed off from the browser tier alone. | **Fixed in round 23** (`desktop-headless-qa`). Keys: `src-tauri/src/secrets.rs` is now the single source for every at-rest secret, and under `--features qa` with `INBORN_QA_KEY_FILE` set it reads them from a file, so a QA build never opens the Keychain and no ACL can be wrong — on any Mac, signed or not. For Moshe's own rebuilds, `scripts/with-signing-identity.sh` (round 21) gives a stable Apple Development requirement; the stale ad-hoc items were deleted once so the signed app owns the ones it creates. Driving: `src-tauri/src/qa.rs`, a QA-only Unix socket (`INBORN_QA_SOCKET`) that evaluates JS in the webview and returns the value, plus `NSApplicationActivationPolicyAccessory` so the app never takes focus; clients `scripts/qa-drive.mjs` and `scripts/qa-desktop-run.mjs`. Both are absent from a shipped build (`strings` on the production binary: 0 hits; on the QA binary: 5). Found on the way: an occluded window gets no animation frames from WebKit, so onboarding S04's *Start* never enabled — the QA plugin now backs `requestAnimationFrame` with `setTimeout` before the app's bundle loads; and QA runs shared `inborn.db` with Moshe's app under a different key, so each wiped the other's chats (QA data now sits beside its key file). Proof, with Moshe working on the same Mac and the window fully hidden behind his own: `docs/qa/desktop-run-2026-09-22.md` — launch, onboarding S01–S05, "The capital of France is Paris." on the Rust Metal engine, the §8.9 sidebar at the default 1120×720 window, and a relaunch after a rebuild with the Keychain item's `mdat` unchanged. Zero `SecurityAgent` events, zero `SecurityAgent` windows, frontmost app never ours. |

### Spec-conformance gaps closed in round 25 (`fixes-r24a`, 22.9.2026)

Seven ranked gaps from `docs/qa/spec-conformance-2026-09-22.md` (#1, #3, #16, #19, #20, #21, #33). Evidence for
every row: `docs/qa/fixes-r24a/`. **No device, emulator, simulator or browser was available to this stream**, so
each row says what is proven by test and what is still unseen on a screen.

| # | Sev | Item | What | Fixed? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F50 | **High** | Family-safe mode (§11.1 Guideline 1.2, §11.2 AI-content, §11.5 item 8) | **We told Apple, Google and our own users that a content filter is on by default, and there was no filter.** Gap 1: the privacy policy §9, the terms §4, `app-privacy-details.md` (the answer to Apple's parental-controls question, literally "Yes: content filter on by default") and our Guideline 1.2 answer all asserted it. `grep` over the tree found no classifier, no mode, no setting and no pref. Four published documents against zero lines of code. | **Fixed in round 25** with a third option, not either of the two the gap listed. `packages/core/src/chat/contentSafety.ts`: a safety clause appended to the system prompt while the mode is on, plus a phrase check run **twice** — over the user's turn, so an explicit request is refused before a token is generated, and over the finished answer, so one that got there anyway is replaced before it is stored. Phrase-shaped patterns in all eight shipped UI languages plus Hebrew, deliberately not single words: a filter that flags "the bombing of Dresden" or "aspirin overdose" gets switched off on day one, and 14 such sentences are pinned as a complement test. Wired into both answer surfaces (`screens/Chat.tsx` catches it mid-stream on the same 8-token beat as the loop detector, so the text does not finish arriving on screen; `voice/useHandsFree.ts` refuses before anything is synthesised aloud). `prefs.contentSafety` defaults **on**, switches in Settings → Chat, and survives an upgrade from a prefs file written before it existed. A replaced row carries `safety: "family-safe"` through all four repositories (memory, SQLite, IndexedDB, Tauri), shows a line under the answer and a row on the ledger. **Not a classifier, and every published sentence now says so** — policy, terms, store answers, §11.1, §11.2, §11.4, §11.5 and edge case 64 were all rewritten to the same words, which is what the gap asked for. 22 tests in `packages/core/test/chat-content-safety.test.ts`. **Unseen on a screen**: the switch, the replaced answer, and what ~245 extra prompt characters do to a real 0.8B model's answers and TTFT. |
| F51 | **High** | The verifiable-client claim (§5.9, §11.3) | **Four shipped places promised a published source that does not exist.** Gap 3: no `LICENSE` file in the repository; the Proof screen's "Source code ↗" button opened `github.com/moshecohen90/inborn`, which 404s because the repository is private; `proof.build.line` told the user to "match it against the published hash" when no hash is published and the build is not reproducible; the terms claimed "parts of Inborn are open source under the MIT license" and "we publish the core precisely so that you can verify our privacy claims"; the privacy policy §10 claimed "a published open-source core"; the Work architecture statement closed on "the app's open-source core and the published build hash allow the claims above to be checked independently". | **Fixed in round 25** by softening all of them to what is true, and adding the license the repository never had. New `LICENSE`: **source-available, not open source** — read, build and publish findings; no redistribution. New `docs/legal/verification.md` is the single place that says what a user can check today (Android permissions on the Play page or by `aapt2`, the iOS App Privacy Report, any firewall, the in-app airplane test) and what they cannot (that a build came from a given tree, that anyone has audited it), plus the wording that is safe to publish. The four texts were rewritten from it; the Proof screen's dead link is replaced by one sentence that says the source is not published and points at CHECK FOR YOURSELF, which needs none of it. A test walks every string in `en.json` and fails on any that offers our source or a hash to match. **This is a decision, not only a fix**: §16 stays open and is in README "Decisions for Moshe" — publish under an open-source license, publish under this source-available one, or drop the claim for good. |
| F52 | Medium | S51 storage + privacy policy §6 | **Android users were told their chats are in the device backup; `allowBackup="false"` means they are not.** Gap 16. `Storage.tsx` showed one sentence on every platform, and `privacy-policy.md:72` repeated it. A user relying on either would reset their phone and lose every chat. | **Fixed in round 25**: `storage.backup.android` in all nine locales, selected by platform, saying plainly that Android backs up nothing the app stores and that deleting the app or resetting the phone ends the chats. The iOS sentence, which is correct, is untouched. The policy now splits the two platforms in the same paragraph and names `android:allowBackup="false"`. §5.3 and edge case 42 amended to match. |
| F53 | Medium | `/voice` (S44) | **The hands-free route was ungated on a release build.** Gap 19: the chat's mic asked `paywallFor(tier, { feature: "voiceConversation" })`, the URL asked nothing, so a deep link, a desktop menu item or a restored route opened a Pro screen for free. | **Fixed in round 25**: `app/voice.tsx` asks the same question the mic asks and returns `<Redirect href="/paywall" />` for a Free tier. It renders nothing while the entitlement is still loading, so there is no frame in which the screen exists before the answer arrives. **Unseen on a screen**: the redirect on a real Free build. |
| F54 | Medium | Model licenses (§11.4, §11.5 item 5) | **We discharged nobody's license obligations.** Gap 20: the Licenses screen showed a name, an attribution line and a link. Apache-2.0 §4(d) and MIT both want the text itself to travel with the distribution, **no LICENSE or NOTICE file was bundled for any model**, and the Hugging Face import showed a license tag and never asked for anything. On an app whose whole promise is that it works with no network, a link discharges nothing. | **Fixed in round 25**: `docs/legal/model-licenses/apache-2.0.txt` (the canonical text, byte for byte from apache.org) and `mit.txt` ship with the app through `packages/core/src/license/licenceTexts.ts`, and a test fails if the module and the files ever drift. A "View license" control in the model card (S31) and on every row of the Licenses screen opens the full scrolling text with no network; MIT's copyright line is filled in per component from `NOTICE.json`, so Phi reads Microsoft's and Whisper reads OpenAI's rather than one generic line. A Hugging Face download now opens the same sheet with an **accept** button and starts only on that tap. A license we ship no text for (an import, a future CC-BY model) says so and offers its link, and a test proves every such entry has an `https` URL to offer. 17 tests in `packages/core/test/license-texts.test.ts` plus the catalogue coverage block in `apps/mobile/test/fixes-r25.test.ts`. **Unseen on a screen**: the sheet itself, and the acceptance before a real download. |
| F55 | Low | S51 storage actions | **Two greyed rows advertised capabilities cut from 1.0**, one of them labelled "Export all (encrypted)" while nothing in the build encrypts an export. Gap 21. | **Fixed in round 25**: both rows removed, and their three strings deleted from all nine locale files rather than left unread — the dead-translation pattern this audit exists to catch. S51's spec entry now says why they are absent. |
| F56 | Low | Crisis card (§8.2 S14, §10.5 #37) | **Korean and Traditional Chinese, two of the eight shipped UI languages, had no crisis phrases at all.** Gap 33: a user writing 죽고 싶어요 or 我不想活了 got no card. | **Fixed in round 25**: phrase lists for `ko` and `zh-Hant`, plus hotlines for KR, TW and HK. **Every number was checked live against findahelpline.com on 22.9.2026, and one was wrong before it shipped**: the draft carried 1577-0199 as Korea's mental-health line, which 109 replaced in 2024 and which is no longer published; it was swapped for 한국생명의전화 1588-9191. 20 tests in `packages/core/test/chat-crisis-languages.test.ts` cover a crisis phrase *and* an ordinary sentence in each of the eight languages, and assert every number in the table is dialable digits. |

**One bug found on the way, outside the seven.** `docs/build.py` still named `docs/spec` and `docs/demo`; the
sources were renamed to `docs/spec-src` and `docs/demo-src` at some point and the script exits on the first
missing file, so **the spec had not been rebuildable from source**. Fixed, and `docs/inborn-spec.html` now
regenerates to the tracked bytes plus this round's amendments.
### New findings (spec conformance sweep, round 26 — 22.9.2026)

Twelve of them, all found by reading `docs/qa/spec-conformance-2026-09-22.md` against the source rather than by
running the app: nine of its ranked gaps (#5, #7, #10, #11, #12, #13, #17, #30, #32) below, and the three incognito
rows F69–F71, which are in their own table further down so the file reads in F order. Eight share one shape — a mechanism that exists, is translated into all eight locales and is read by nothing —
which is why every fix here ships with a test that fails when the wiring is removed, not only when the logic is.
Evidence: `docs/qa/fixes-r24b/tests.txt` (green run) and `docs/qa/fixes-r24b/sabotage.txt` (every guard watched
going red with its fix taken out, one at a time). Round 26 detail: `README.md` section "Fixes round 26".

| # | Sev | Item | What | Fixed? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F57 | Medium | Clipboard expiry (§7.5, gap #5) | **A shipped Settings security control that did nothing.** `Settings.tsx` offered Off / 60 s and wrote `prefs.clipboardExpirySec`; `lib/clipboard.ts` ignored it and its own comment deferred expiry to M6. A user who turned it on got no expiry and no sign that nothing happened. | **Fixed.** `apps/mobile/src/lib/clipboardExpiry.ts`: `ExpiringClipboard` over an injectable pasteboard and timer, wired in `clipboard.ts` for both the native and the web/Tauri pasteboard, and fed from `AppServices` so every copy site inherits the setting. It never wipes text it did not put there — it reads the pasteboard back first, and a pasteboard it cannot read is left alone. A second copy replaces the first timer; setting Off releases a copy already on the clock. 8 tests (`clipboardExpiry.test.ts`). **Not proven on a device**: the iOS and Android pasteboards themselves are the platform's, and this stream ran no phone. |
| F58 | **High** | Database corruption (§10.3 case 22, gap #7) | **Corruption was answered by deletion.** `sqliteRepository.ts` caught "not a database" and called `deleteDatabaseAsync` — silent, total, irreversible loss of every chat on an app whose whole promise is that chats live only here. The desktop carried the same three lines (`store.rs`, `remove_file` over the database and its WAL). A file that opened but failed `quick_check` was never noticed at all, and `T40` never ran. | **Fixed.** Core owns the pure part (`packages/core/src/storage/integrity.ts`): which SQLite errors mean the file is unusable, how to read `quick_check`, and a salvage that reads a table by row id and retries a failed page row by row, so only the rows a broken page actually holds are lost. The phones now quarantine the old file under `inborn.db.corrupt-<stamp>` (one kept copy at a time), rebuild into a fresh keyed file — FTS and the shared document index included, parents copied before children so an orphan is counted rather than smuggled in — and promote it. The desktop quarantines instead of deleting and reports it through `db_open`. Either way the §8.8 strip tells the user, in all eight languages (`state.dbRepaired` with the count that was lost, `state.dbStartedFresh`). 7 core tests against a real SQLite through sql.js, 6 mobile tests, 2 Rust tests. **Not proven on a device or on the desktop app**: no emulator was free to this stream and no Tauri build was run; the file moves themselves (`dbFile.native.ts`) are covered by review only. |
| F59 | Medium | First automatic switch (§8.8 row 4c, gap #10) | **The explainer sheet was never built.** `policy.ts` computed `rec.explain`, `guard.ts` latched it, `ackExplain()` existed to dismiss it and four `device.sheet.*` keys shipped in all eight locales — and no component read any of it. The first time the app took the model away from the user, it said nothing. Worse, the latch was spent by any status change at all, so by the time a component did read it, it would already have been used up by a memory eviction. | **Fixed.** `components/shell/DeviceExplainSheet.tsx` renders the four shipped strings and offers "Don't switch automatically", which writes the `neverSwitchModel` override the guard already had; mounted app-wide from `app/_layout.tsx` beside the §8.8 strip. `GuardState.explain` now carries which tier it switched from and to instead of a bare flag, and `policy.ts` only spends the once-per-install latch on a switch that has a target tier. 5 wiring tests (`device/explainSheetWired.test.ts`) plus 2 in `device-policy.test.ts`. **Not photographed**: the sheet renders in a component, and vitest here is node-only. |
| F60 | Medium | Wi-Fi-only downloads (§8.4, §10.1 case 4, gap #11) | **Two toggles, two stores, zero readers.** `shouldWait()` had no production call site at all: a user on cellular with the switch on still pulled 1.2 GB. Android looked correct only because Play enforces its own policy; iOS and the desktop had nothing. | **Fixed.** One preference (`prefs.wifiOnly`, the field dropped from `VaultRecord`), pushed into the vault by `AppServices` the same way the device-guard override is, and read by the one place that downloads: `httpsDelivery.ts` now parks before each shard while `shouldWait(bytes, network, wifiOnly)` is true, emits `waiting-for-wifi` once, polls `expo-network` every 5 s, and wakes immediately on Pause or Cancel instead of leaving a timer running. An unreported network type counts as cellular, because a VPN or a tether bills like one. 6 delivery tests plus the core rule's own. **Not proven on a phone**: no device was available to this stream, so the `expo-network` reading itself is unverified. |
| F61 | Medium | Inference queue (§10.3 case 23, gap #12) | **Two generations could reach the same context.** `adapters/llamaRn.ts` overwrote `inflight` per call, and only per-surface `busy` flags stood between a chat turn, the documents ask and a quick action — a plausible native crash with no test anywhere. | **Fixed.** `packages/core/src/llm/inferenceQueue.ts`: one turn at a time, in the order they asked, with `waiting`/`depth`/`busy` for the UI and an `AbortSignal` that takes a turn out of the queue before it ever reaches the context. `engine.ts` holds one queue for the process and both `generate` and `benchmarkModel` go through it; a failed turn hands the slot straight back rather than wedging the chain. 7 tests, one of which shows the same two calls overlapping without the queue. |
| F62 | Low | AI Act export stamp (§11.3 Art. 50(2), gap #13) | **The Work signed record, the primary artifact of that export path, carried no AI marking** while Markdown, JSON and text exports all did. The unit test `ai-act-notes.md` promised did not exist. | **Fixed.** `work/signedRecord.ts` writes `aiGenerated: true` and a `generator` naming the app, both inside the hashed content, so stripping either one yields `hash-mismatch` rather than a quietly unmarked record. Pinned in `packages/core/test/work-signed.test.ts`. |
| F63 | Low | Desktop minimum window (§9.7, gap #17) | **The window could shrink below the size the desktop shell needs.** `tauri.conf.json` said 720×480 while `lib/layout.ts` defined `DESKTOP_MIN = 1040` and quoted the spec at it. | **Fixed.** 1040×720, and `lib/layout.test.ts` now reads `tauri.conf.json` itself: every window's minimum must be exactly the constant the layout code uses, its opening size must be at least its minimum, and that width must classify as `desktop`. Two numbers can drift again; the test cannot. |
| F64 | Medium | Desktop drag-and-drop (§8.9, gap #30) | **Dropped files went nowhere.** `shell.rs` emitted `inborn:documents-dropped` and `tauri.ts` relayed it to a listener that did not exist. Folders were not handled at all, so a dropped folder reached the web layer as a path with no extension and imported as nothing. | **Fixed.** Rust walks a dropped folder (4 levels, 64 files, hidden entries and `.gguf` kept out of the document path) and hands back real files; a new `documents_read` command refuses any path this window did not itself hand over and anything over 200 MB, so the webview gained no filesystem. On the JS side a pure planner (`documents/dropped.ts`) sniffs the real first bytes, applies the tier's attachment limit and the Work gate, and the Documents screen imports what it accepts through the existing import path while naming what it skipped. 7 planner tests, 3 Rust tests. **Not proven by an actual drag**: a real OS drop needs a pointer, which this stream may not use. |
| F65 | Medium | Partial answer on a kill (§8.8 row 5a, gap #32) | **A jetsam kill lost the answer on screen**, because the assistant row was written only after the stream ended. | **Fixed.** `lib/partialAnswer.ts` says when the text so far is due to be written (first text at once, then once per 1.5 s, never twice for the same text, and never again after a store refuses); `Chat.tsx` writes it through as a stopped row and re-points at the row the store returned, so the final write updates it rather than appending a second one. A store that fails is not retried for that answer. `MessagePatch.stoppedBy` now accepts `null` so an answer that finishes after all clears the flag — in the in-memory, IndexedDB and SQLite repositories alike, pinned in the shared repository contract test. 5 tests plus 1 contract test across three repositories. |

### Three gaps from the spec-conformance audit, closed in round 27 (stream `fixes-r25`, 22.9.2026)

Not found by a test run: these are gaps #15, #18 and #6 of `docs/qa/spec-conformance-2026-09-22.md`, filed here so
each one has a row with its proof. All three share a shape — a promise written in a shipped document, and a number
or a switch in generated or disabled code that nobody ever compared it against.

| # | Sev | Item | What | Fixed? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F66 | **High** | Lifecycle, §10.3 case 21 | **The 15-second background grace was not real on iOS, and the pass bar said it was.** §10.3 #21 and §6.5 promise that an answer still streaming when the app leaves the screen gets up to 15 s to finish, then keeps its partial text and offers Continue. iOS suspends a process within seconds of it leaving the screen, so on a phone the budget simply stopped being spent: `grep beginBackgroundTask` over the whole tree returned nothing, and `release-checklist.md:101` (T12) stated a pass bar — "iOS finishes up to 15 s then saves partial and shows Continue" — that no build could meet. The same T12 row demanded of Android a "short-lived foreground service with notification" and told the tester to verify it stopped with `adb shell dumpsys activity services`; `grep FOREGROUND_SERVICE` finds the permission only as a Play asset-delivery transitive, and there is no service of ours to find. | **Fixed in round 27** (`fixes-r25`). **iOS**: `apps/mobile/modules/background-task/` wraps UIApplication's background task, and `apps/mobile/src/device/bgHold.ts` decides when one is held from the only two facts that decide it — off-screen, and still generating. `guard.ts` syncs it from the three events it already subscribes to (app state, engine activity, expiry), so a hold cannot outlive the answer it was taken for. When iOS reclaims its time before the grace is up the answer stops with its partial text kept and is reported as the same pause the grace raises, so the chat offers Continue through `wasStoppedByGuard()` / `notePausedTurn` — the path that already existed, not a second one. Asking for more time in the background episode the OS just reclaimed, which is how an app gets killed, is latched off until the app is in front again. The native side claims the token before `beginBackgroundTask` can expire it, so the race where the expiration handler finds nothing to end cannot leave a task running and the app killed for it. 8 tests (`apps/mobile/src/device/bgHold.test.ts`). **Built for the real phone**: an unsigned arm64 `iphoneos` build (`pod install` + `xcodebuild build`, exit 0, 0 errors) carries the Objective-C class `_TtC14BackgroundTask20BackgroundTaskModule` and the selectors `beginBackgroundTaskWithName:expirationHandler:`, `endBackgroundTask:` and `backgroundTimeRemaining` in `Inborn.debug.dylib` — the same grep that returned nothing over the whole tree — and Expo's autolinking registered the module itself in `ExpoModulesProvider.swift` (`docs/qa/fixes-r25/ios-device-build.txt`). **Not proven**: the hold's runtime effect on a backgrounded phone. Driving the iPhone needs the "Enter iPhone Passcode for 'XCTest' · Enable UI Automation" sheet, which only Moshe can accept and whose grant does not persist (`ios-device-pass-12-2026-09-22.md`, 14:42), so T12 stays open for the next pass he is present for. **Android: no foreground service, decided rather than deferred.** Three reasons. (1) Android does not suspend the process when the app leaves the screen; it pauses JS *timers*, which is exactly why the grace has always been enforced on the token stream in `engine.ts` (`setPauseCheck`, asked per streamed token) and not on a `setTimeout`. (2) It is proven working without one: release-checklist T13, 13.9.2026, `KEYCODE_SLEEP` for 25 s on the E-P6-36 emulator produced "Paused while Inborn was in the background. The partial answer is kept." with CONTINUE, the partial in the DB, and CONTINUE resumed (`shots/35-t13-paused-banner.png`, `36-t13-continued.png`). (3) The cost is real and buys nothing: a foreground service puts a persistent notification on screen for a fifteen-second window, and since Android 14 a `dataSync` service needs a Play Console declaration with a video justification and carries a 6 h/24 h budget — for a promise already kept. The residual risk a service would cover is a low-memory kill of a backgrounded app, which is gap #32 (the partial answer is written only when the stream ends) and is filed separately. T12's pass bar and the §10.3 #21 row now say this. **Proof**: `docs/qa/fixes-r25/`. |
| F67 | Medium | iOS deployment target (§6.3) | **Every build shipped at iOS 16.4 while four documents declared iOS 17.** `Podfile:27` fell through to Expo's own 16.4 default and all four `IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET` entries in the generated pbxproj read 16.4, against §6.3, `release-checklist.md:246` (T36 tests the floor at iOS 17), `privacy-policy.md:39` and the store listing. `ios/` is generated and gitignored, so no reviewer and no test ever saw the number that shipped; the App Store's own record for builds 6 through 12 reads `minOsVersion 16.4`. Two of the podspecs we own were lower still — `SecureScreen.podspec` at 15.1. | **Fixed in round 27** (`fixes-r25`) using the key Expo already owns rather than a plugin of our own: `ios.deploymentTarget: "17.0"` in `app.config.ts`, which `@expo/prebuild-config`'s default chain applies to `ios/Podfile.properties.json` and to the app target's build configurations. It leaves the *project-level* pair at the template's 16.4, which any target added later would inherit, so `plugins/withProjectDeploymentTarget.js` carries the same number down to them. **Proved twice, by running the build rather than reading the config**: all four pbxproj entries and the Podfile property read 17.0 after prebuild (`docs/qa/fixes-r25/ios-deployment-target.txt`), and the `Info.plist` of the arm64 `iphoneos` app that comes out of `xcodebuild` reads `MinimumOSVersion 17.0` (`docs/qa/fixes-r25/ios-device-build.txt`), against the `minOsVersion 16.4` App Store Connect recorded for builds 6 through 12. Every podspec we own says 17.0. 3 tests (`apps/mobile/test/iosDeploymentTarget.test.ts`) hold the config, the podspecs and the two documents to one number, so the next drift fails on a laptop instead of in App Store Connect. |
| F68 | **High** | CI, §5.1 zero-INTERNET gate | **The gate the whole privacy claim rests on was switched off.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` carried `android-permission-gate: if: false` with a TODO saying it needed a release APK. So the one check behind decision D3 — never `android.permission.INTERNET` — was a human remembering to run a script, on an artifact nobody else could see, while two store questionnaires, the privacy policy and the app's central promise assert the result. Worse for a guard: it had only ever been watched pass. | **Fixed in round 27** (`fixes-r25`). Nothing has to be built: the merged release manifest is the artifact that decides the answer, and gradle writes it from `:app:processReleaseManifest` with no signing key, no models and no build-tools. `scripts/check-android-permissions.sh` now takes that file alongside an APK or AAB, and **refuses a pre-merge manifest** rather than passing it — `app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml` lists INTERNET as `tools:node="remove"` and nothing else outside the allowlist, so a gate pointed at it would go green while no dependency manifest had been merged yet; the merger's injected `<uses-sdk>` and dropped `tools` namespace tell the two apart. **Watched fail on the real path**: the same three commands with `APP_VARIANT=development`, which empties `android.blockedPermissions`, produce a manifest carrying 18 permissions with INTERNET among them and the gate exits 1. That listing also settles gap #25: the INTERNET does come from the `openiap-google` wrapper, so the spec's stated reason ("the Billing library's manifest has no INTERNET") is wrong and `blockedPermissions` is what removes it — along with eight others. 5 tests (`apps/mobile/test/androidPermissionGate.test.ts`) covering the green case, INTERNET, an undocumented permission, the pre-merge near-miss and a missing file. Green run, red run and the CI job: `docs/qa/fixes-r25/ci-permission-gate.txt`. |

### Incognito, round 26 (stream `fixes-r24b`, 22.9.2026)

The three incognito rows of the same sweep. §5.7 promises an incognito chat leaves nothing behind; what the user
attached to it did not keep that promise. Fix, evidence and sabotage as above.

| # | Sev | Item | What | Fixed? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F69 | **High** | Incognito documents (§5.7) | **A document added inside an incognito chat was written to the encrypted library on disk** — row, chunks and vectors — and stayed there after the session. The chat itself was RAM-only; what the user attached to it was not. | **Fixed.** `packages/core/src/rag/splitStore.ts`: one `EmbeddingStore` over two, routed per document id, decided by the caller at import and never guessed from the record. The library keeps a RAM store for incognito documents and answers a single question from both, so the session still works while it lasts. 6 core tests, and a mobile test that drives the real import-and-index path and asserts the saved store never saw the document. |
| F70 | Medium | Incognito session end (§5.7) | **Nothing ever ended a session.** `ChatStore.endSession()` shipped with no production caller, and the document library had no equivalent at all, so incognito chats and their documents survived in RAM for the whole app run. | **Fixed.** `DocumentLibrary.endSession()` cancels indexing, deletes the copied file, drops the record, detaches it from every chat and clears the RAM store; `AppServices.closeActive` calls both it and `ChatStore.endSession()` on every path that leaves a chat. |
| F71 | Low | Incognito share-in (§5.7) | **A share arriving during an incognito session opened a saved chat**, silently taking the conversation out of incognito. | **Fixed.** `openShared` keeps the current incognito state instead of hard-coding `false`. Covered with F70 by `services/incognitoExit.test.ts`, which reads the wiring `AppServices` cannot be rendered to prove. |

### The §7 tier gates, from the spec-conformance audit (22.9.2026)

All five come from `docs/qa/spec-conformance-2026-09-22.md` — the "§7 — Feature matrix" section and its eleven-row
tier-mismatch list. Evidence for every row below: `docs/qa/fixes-r24c/`.

| # | Sev | Item | What | Fixed? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F72 | **High** | Share target (§7.7) vs the document gates (§7.3) | **A file shared in from another app walked past both document gates.** `Chat.tsx:710-722` took each file out of the share payload and called `library.importFile` directly — no `paywallFor`, no office-kind check — while the attach sheet's own "Add a file" went through `pickIntoLibrary`, which asked both. Two consequences, both of them the paid product given away through the cheapest surface in the app: a Free user, whose cap is **one** file per chat (§7.3 row 1, `limits().filesPerChat`), could share in ten files from Files and get ten attached; and a Free **or Pro** user could share in an `.xlsx` or an `.html`, which §7.3 row 8 sells as Work and which the picker refuses with the `office-work-card` value moment. The share target is also the cheapest acquisition surface we have (§7.7 "משטח רכישה זול"), so this is the door most likely to be used. Worse than the picker in one respect: a shared file arrives with a **sender-supplied name**, and `.docx` and `.xlsx` are the same ZIP magic bytes, so an Excel file shared without an extension would not have been recognised even if the check had been there. | **Fixed in round 28** (`fixes-r24c`). The gates moved into one pure function rather than being copied into a second door: `packages/core/src/license/intake.ts` `fileIntake(tier, kind, attachedCount)` answers `ok`, `paywall/document` or `paywall/office` for **every** file entering the library, and `importPicker.ts`, `DocumentsScreen.tsx` and the share path in `Chat.tsx` all call it. The count moment is answered before the kind, so a Free user's second spreadsheet opens the paywall on the cap it actually hit. The name problem is answered by `sharedName()` in `documents/office.ts`, which runs the sender's name and MIME type through the existing `pickedFileName` (the same helper that solved the SAF-id problem in QA F3), so an extensionless share of `application/vnd.openxmlformats-officedocument.spreadsheetml.sheet` resolves to `blob.xlsx` and is refused. A blocked share now flashes the reason and opens the paywall instead of failing silently: `quick.filePro`, `quick.fileWork`, both in all 8 locales. **Proof**: 11 assertions in `packages/core/test/license-intake.test.ts`, three of which feed the exact payload shapes `parseSharePayload` produces on Android and iOS through the whole name → sniff → gate chain; plus a source-scan guard, `apps/mobile/test/gates-wired.test.ts`, that fails if any module calls `library.importFile` outside the three doors. The guard was watched failing with the fix removed — `docs/qa/fixes-r24c/guard-red-share-target.txt`. **Not proven on a device**: no phone, emulator or browser was used in this round. |
| F73 | Medium | OCR and strict mode (§7.3 rows 3 and 4) | **Two Pro rows of the feature matrix shipped with no gate at all.** On-device OCR for scans and images is §7.3 row 3, Pro, and the audit calls it "a magnet for Work (receipts, contracts)". `DocumentsScreen.tsx:237` wired the row's action straight to `library.runOcr` and the only condition anywhere was `Platform.OS !== "web"`. "Answer only from my documents" is §7.3 row 4, Pro; its switch was open on **two** surfaces, the library screen and the attach sheet, and — the part a UI-only gate would have missed — the value is read at prompt time from `state.strict`, so a user who turned it on while subscribed kept the Pro answering behaviour after the license lapsed. | **Fixed in round 28**: the dead `ocr` key is wired (`ocrLocked` in `DocumentsScreen.tsx`, the row's action opens the paywall and reads `Run OCR · PRO`), and a new `strictDocuments: "pro"` key gates both switches. The switch gate is the visible half; the enforcing half is one line in `documents/hooks.ts`, where `strict` is masked by the tier before it reaches `library.ask`, `buildPrompt` and the chat's system context — so a lapsed license stops changing answers, not just the chrome. Memory's read path got the same treatment (F74). **Proof**: `license-gates.test.ts` pins both keys to `pro`; `gates-wired.test.ts` fails if either goes dead again. **Not proven on a device.** |
| F74 | Medium | Memory (§7.6) | **Memory was Pro only against the "Add" button.** `MemorySheet.tsx` gated `memory-add` and showed a `PRO` chip, and left everything else open: editing an existing fact, the master switch, the per-persona switches, and the enabled toggle on each fact. §7.6 sells "on-device memory ('facts about me'), viewable / editable / deletable, per persona" as one Pro row. The gap only matters for a user who had facts and then lost the license — a lapsed or refunded Pro — but for that user nothing changed at all: `store.memoryFor()` kept handing the facts to the model on every turn, because the call site asked no tier question. | **Fixed in round 28**, with a line drawn rather than a blanket gate: every **write** is Pro (add, edit, turning memory on, turning a persona's memory on, re-enabling a fact), and every **delete** stays free — deleting your own data is a privacy control, and §7 opens by saying an app that charges for privacy gets called a scam in its reviews. Turning memory **off** is likewise free; only turning it on is sold. The read path is gated at the source: `Chat.tsx` asks `can("memory")` before `store.memoryFor`, so a lapsed license stops the model seeing the facts without deleting them. **Not proven on a device.** |
| F75 | Low | The ledger (§7.1 and §7.8) | **Every answer shipped the full Pro statistics receipt to Free users.** §7.1 gives Free a ledger of four named fields — model, quantization, context, ms/token — and §7.8 sells "per-message statistics (tok/s, TTFT, tokens, energy estimate)" as Free basic · **Pro detailed**. `Ledger.tsx` rendered all eight rows unconditionally, and `detailedStats` was one of the 23 gate keys with no call site anywhere. | **Fixed in round 28**: `FREE_LEDGER_ROWS` in `packages/core/src/license/gates.ts` is the §7.1 list, quoted from the spec row by row, and `Ledger.tsx` filters by it unless `can("detailedStats")`. A Free user sees the four rows plus one `More detail · PRO` line that opens the paywall; nothing is hidden behind a blank. **Proof**: `license-gates.test.ts` asserts the four rows and, as the complement, that none of the four detail rows is in that list. **Not proven on a device.** |
| F76 | Medium | `license/gates.ts` (§7.9) and `docs/build.py` | **23 of 39 gate keys had no call site, and nothing would ever have told us.** This is F42's shape in the licensing layer: a machine-readable §7 matrix, complete and typed, most of which no screen ever asked. Roughly half the dead keys map to capabilities `README.md:596-599` declares "intentionally not built for 1.0", so they were gates for features that do not exist; the rest gated features the matrix still sells. **Found on the way**: `docs/build.py` could not build the spec at all. Commit `077aacf` renamed `docs/spec` to `docs/spec-src` and the script's `SPEC` constant was never updated, so `python3 docs/build.py` exited with `missing …/docs/spec/00-head.html`. Every instruction that ends "then run `docs/build.py`" has been unrunnable since that rename. | **Fixed in round 28.** The 11 keys for declared cuts are **deleted** — `encryptedBackup`, `deviceTransfer`, `keyboardExtension`, `lanConnection`, `advancedControls`, `gpuTuning`, `speculativeDecoding`, `multiModel`, `compareModels`, `localServer`, `personaWidgets` — and return with their features. Three were wired by this round (`ocr`, `detailedStats`, and the new `strictDocuments`). The 11 that remain unbuilt but are still sold by §7 are named in one exported list, `UNBUILT_FEATURES`, and `apps/mobile/test/gates-wired.test.ts` now fails if a key is neither called by a screen nor on that list — and equally if a key **on** the list turns out to be enforced. That is the guard the audit's gap 40 asked for, and it was watched failing with one unwired key added back: `docs/qa/fixes-r24c/guard-red-dead-key.txt`. `docs/build.py` now points at `spec-src` and rebuilds; it still writes into `docs/out/`, so publishing `docs/inborn-spec.html` is a copy, and the demo half of the script (`ROOT/demo`, also renamed to `demo-src`) is still skipped silently — left for whoever owns the demo. |

### New findings (desktop pass 1 — §8.9 / §9.7 against the running Mac app, 22.9.2026)

The first pass to drive the Tauri app itself rather than a browser at desktop widths. Channel and method:
`docs/qa/desktop-pass-1/README.md`, readings in `docs/qa/desktop-pass-1/run.json`. Moshe was working on the
same Mac throughout — no dialog, no pointer, no focus change. A fourth filed row, the dropped document that
went nowhere, was **withdrawn** when this branch merged `origin/main`: F64 had already built that path.

| # | Sev | Item | What | Fixed? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F47 | Medium | Desktop window minimum (§9.7) | **The window opened, and reopened, below the minimum the spec gives it.** §9.7 sets 1,040×720 and `lib/layout.ts:7` even names it `DESKTOP_MIN`, but `apps/desktop/src-tauri/tauri.conf.json` said `minWidth: 720, minHeight: 480`. Asked for 700×460 the app took it exactly, and at that size drew the **phone shell** — no sidebar, the header's "Chats" button the only door to the chat list (`docs/qa/desktop-pass-1/00-f47-before-700x460-was-accepted.png`). The config half of this is **F63's, not this branch's**: `origin/main` raised those two numbers in round 26 and pinned them in `lib/layout.test.ts`, and this branch merged it. What is filed here is the half that change cannot reach. AppKit's `contentMinSize` constrains a drag and not a size set in code, and `tauri-plugin-window-state` restores the last size by setting it in code — **after** `setup` and after `RunEvent::Ready` both, which is where the first two attempts at this fix sat and did nothing. Every install already carries a state file written under the old 720×480 minimum, so those windows would have gone on opening under the new one for ever. | **Fixed on `desktop-pass-1`**: `shell.rs::hold_to_configured_minimum` hangs off the window's own `Resized` event, reads the minimum from the same config F63's test asserts, and grows a window that is under it — so the raised number also reaches a window saved under the old one. Proven from a state file holding 500×400: the app logged `window was 500x400, under the 1040x720 minimum; grown` and came up at 1040×720 with the sidebar at 280 and the column at 680 (`03-f47-at-the-1040x720-minimum.png`). 2 tests in `shell.rs`, and two assertions added to F63's own test in `lib/layout.test.ts` for what it left uncovered: one pixel under the minimum is not the desktop mode, and §8.9's sidebar fits inside it with the column and with the panel. **Not proven**: that AppKit refuses a *drag* below the minimum — that needs a pointer. |
| F48 | **High** | Desktop shell (§8.9) | **The desktop shell never followed the window.** §8.9's sidebar, 680 px column, document panel and palette routing all switch on `useWindowDimensions().width`, and in the Tauri WKWebView that number was frozen at whatever the window was on the first paint. Measured with counters armed in the page, over five resizes (1400 → 760 → 700 → 1200 → 1400): `window.innerWidth` and `visualViewport.width` tracked the window exactly, **`window` fired 0 resize events and `visualViewport` fired 0**, and the 280 px sidebar was still there at an `innerWidth` of 700 — the wide shell in a window 60 px below the phone threshold. Two things had to be true at once: the WKWebView delivers no resize event for a Tauri window resize, and react-native-web 0.21.2's `Dimensions` subscribes to **`visualViewport`'s** resize event whenever that object exists and to `window`'s only when it does not, so even a `resize` event dispatched by hand was ignored; `Dimensions.get()` refreshes once behind a `shouldInit` flag and never again. Round 22 could not have caught this: it proved §8.9 in headless Chromium, where `resize` fires normally. | **Fixed on `desktop-pass-1`**: the one event the window does deliver is Tauri's own `tauri://resize`, so `adapters/tauri.ts` now drives `lib/viewportResize.ts::dispatchViewportResize` from it, notifying whichever target this browser's `Dimensions` subscribed to. After the fix three resizes produced three `visualViewport` resize events and the shell was right at 1600, 1040 and 1280 — 280 px sidebar and 680 px column each time, 340 px panel at 1400 (`04-document-panel-1400x900.png`). 3 tests in `apps/mobile/src/lib/viewportResize.test.ts`. |
| F49 | Medium | Desktop keyboard (§8.9, §9.7) | **Esc did not stop a running answer.** `matchKey` maps Escape to `stop` and `_layout.tsx:74` calls Esc "Stop" in a comment, but `useDesktopKeys` returned early on `isTauri()` — the whole key map, so the native menu's accelerators would not fire twice. macOS reserves Esc and no accelerator can carry it, so the menu binds Stop to ⌘. and Esc reached nothing at all. Measured on one generation each, same prompt: after **Esc** the stop button was still showing 3.5 s later and the answer had grown from 506 to 3,342 characters; after the menu's **Stop** it was gone inside 500 ms with the text frozen at 5,033. | **Fixed on `desktop-pass-1`**: the decision is now `webviewShortcut(e, menuOwnsAccelerators)` in `lib/desktopKeys.ts` — under Tauri the webview keeps Esc and drops every key the menu declares. Esc now stops a streaming answer within 700 ms and also closes the command palette (`06-f49-escape-stopped-the-answer.png`). 3 tests in `desktopKeys.test.ts`; removing the guard turns the double-fire test red. |
| F81 | Medium | Desktop settings (§8.9) | **Settings has none of the rows §8.9 asks the desktop for.** No model location — the app knows it (`desktop_info` returns `…/com.inbornapp.desktop/models`) and shows the phone's "Storage location · Internal" instead; no "Launch at login (off)"; and no Advanced section at all, so no backend picker (Auto/CUDA/Vulkan/Metal/CPU), no VRAM offload and no local server (Pro). Confirms the audit row at runtime on the app itself. | No — a screen's worth of new surface, and Launch at login needs an autostart plugin. The backend cannot become a picker while it is a compile-time Cargo feature (`engine.rs:22-28`; this build reports `metal`). |
| F82 | Medium | Desktop paywall (§8.9, §12.4) | **The desktop paywall offers two priced buttons that cannot be pressed.** "Unlock Work · $69.99" and "Unlock Pro · $19.99" both render `aria-disabled="true"`, because the desktop provider is `license-key` and `PaywallScreen.tsx:121` disables every buy button in that case. The only live door is "Have a license key?". The Work-first ordering §8.9 asks for is correct (`tier-inborn.work` then `tier-inborn.pro`), so today it orders two dead cards first. | No — what should stand there instead (a website checkout link, or no CTA at all) is a copy and commerce decision. |
| F83 | Low | Desktop settings copy | **The native macOS app says "Not available in a browser."** The screenshot-protection row picks its sub-line from `Platform.OS`, which is `"web"` under Tauri, so it lands on `settings.security.screenshots.web` (`Settings.tsx:118`). A user of the Mac app is told about a browser. | No — one `isTauri()` branch plus a new string in eight locales; the translations belong to the translation owner. |

**9da93a2**) on **Moshe's OnePlus 6T** (`<6t-serial>`, Android 11, 8 GB, tier `ANDROID-LEGACY`) running Play internal
**1.0.0 (16)**, delivered as an update in place over vc15, plus the `Pixel_6_API_33` emulator (`emulator-5554`,
4 GB, tier `ANDROID-ENTRY`) carrying the same AAB through `bundletool --local-testing`. Keys only on the phone.

Release rows, build, upload, Play billing and the device-tier rows are **section S** of
`docs/qa/purchases-run-2026-09-11.md`; the hour of continuous use is `docs/qa/soak-run-9-2026-09-22.md`; evidence
index in `docs/qa/android-vc16/README.md`.

**No finding was filed.** Nothing in this pass is a defect in the app, so no F number was taken. The one behaviour
that looked like one — Fast drawn but with no install action on the 4 GB emulator — is the catalog doing what it
says: `fast.minRamGB` is **6**, `pick.ts ramFit()` returns `"no"` below it, and `ModelCard.tsx` renders
`vault.willNotRun` in place of the speed line. Deliberate, and the same treatment the two Sharp cards get.

### The row this pass closes

| # | Row | Device | Result | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 8a | **§5.7 incognito: chat rows never written to the DB or the search index** — `spec-conformance-2026-09-22.md` has this **UNPROVEN**: "Correct in code; simulator and emulator only, never a real device" | OnePlus 6T, Play 1.0.0 (16) | **PASS on real hardware.** Paired run: `new-chat` opens the sheet with `incognito-switch` `checked="false"`, `new-incognito` with `checked="true"`; both chats answer a real turn (header `INCOGNITO · NOT SAVED`); both appear in the chat list while the incognito session is alive; after `am force-stop` + relaunch the normal chat survives (`KESTREL` 1 row) and the incognito chat is gone (`ZARFOLIN` **0 rows**) | `docs/qa/android-vc16/g-02…g-05`, §S rows S11–S13 |

The pairing is the point. The first attempt returned **INCONCLUSIVE** and was discarded: both codewords sat at the
end of the prompt and the chat list truncates its title to the first few words, so *neither* chat could be found and
"the incognito chat is not in the list" would have been true whatever the app did. A negative row needs a positive
control that actually comes back.

What this does **not** close: `chat/store.ts endSession()` still has zero callers, so the guarantee observed here is
delivered by the process exiting. Closing an incognito chat while the app keeps running is untested, and the audit's
separate **MISSING** row — an incognito-attached document is still indexed into the SQLCipher store — is untouched
by this pass.

### Two release-pipeline failures, found by the build-13 run (22.9.2026)

Neither is an app defect and neither reached a user; both cost this run a failed step and both will recur, so they are
filed rather than left in a build log. Evidence and the exact commands: `docs/qa/ios-build-13-2026-09-22.md`.

| # | Sev | Item | What | Fixed? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F90 | Medium | iOS release (process) | **`-exportArchive` dies on a stale locked keychain, and the error does not say so.** The first export ended `error: exportArchive codesign command failed (…/ExpoModulesJSI.framework: errSecInternalComponent)` with no cause named. The distribution identity this step re-signs with lives **only** in Xcode's temporary `~/Library/Keychains/signing_temp.keychain-db`; a stale copy left behind by an earlier unfinished pipeline (mtime 18:22) was sitting **first** in the user's keychain search list and was **locked** — `security show-keychain-info` answers "Unable to obtain authorization", while `login.keychain-db` was unlocked and `no-timeout` the whole time. `codesign` therefore chose an identity it could not use. Nothing about the archive was wrong: the identical command succeeded once the dead keychain was out of the way. | **Worked around in this run, not fixed in code.** `security list-keychains -s ~/Library/Keychains/login.keychain-db /Library/Keychains/System.keychain` and re-run: Xcode then created its own temporary keychain, fetched the cloud-managed distribution certificate into it, signed, and **deleted the file afterwards**, so the search list was left at `login` + `System`, which is the correct default. A copy of the stale keychain was kept in the session scratch dir first; nothing in the login keychain was read or altered and no password dialog was raised. The durable fix would be for the iOS release script to check the search list before it starts and refuse a locked keychain by name. |
| F91 | Medium | iOS release (process) | **`scripts/asc-key-env.sh --cleanup` deletes every stream's staged key, not its own.** `altool --validate-app` succeeded at 18:51:12; the `--upload-app` that ran one second later failed with `Failed to load AuthKey file. (-43) The file AuthKey_<asc-key-id>.p8 could not be found`. The key had been staged for the whole pipeline and was still present for the validate. `--cleanup` runs `find "$dir" -name 'AuthKey_*.p8' -delete`, which takes **any** stream's staged key, so a concurrent iOS run finishing inside that 61-second window disarms the one in flight. | **Worked around, not fixed.** This run re-stages the key immediately before each `altool` call (`scratchpad/ios-build-13/upload-only.sh`), and the repeated upload succeeded on the first try. The real fix is one line — have `--cleanup` delete only the file whose key id the caller staged — and it belongs with whoever owns `scripts/asc-key-env.sh`. |
