From MosheAI's sixth verdict §3. Static: no device, emulator or browser. Evidence: `docs/qa/fixes-r26/`.

| # | Sev | Item | What | Fixed? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F92 | **Blocker** | Legal screen / site | **Twelve raw `{{…}}` placeholders render to the user, inside TestFlight 13 and Play vc17.** `Legal.tsx` imports `docs/legal/privacy-policy.md` and `terms.md` and renders the body as it is, so `{{DOMAIN}}`×5, `{{SUPPORT_EMAIL}}`×2, `{{DEVELOPER_LEGAL_NAME}}`, `{{POSTAL_ADDRESS}}` and three more in the terms reached both binaries; `apps/site/dist/privacy.html` also carried "Status: DRAFT. Not yet published". | **Fixed.** Every token filled (support@inbornapp.com, inbornapp.com, https://inbornapp.com/privacy, Israeli law with exclusive jurisdiction in Israel, 22 September 2026, Cohen Apps) in `privacy-policy.md`, `terms.md`, `app-privacy-details.md`, `licenses.md`, `NOTICE.json`; both DRAFT lines removed. Identity per Moshe, 22.9: the Tanach apps' shape — `{{POSTAL_ADDRESS}}` deleted rather than filled, contact is the support address and +1-440-847-8502, with no physical reception; a test fails if an address comes back. `{{COPYRIGHT}}` stays in the model-license texts, where `licenceText()` fills it. Proof: `F92-web-bundle.txt` (built bundle holds `{{COPYRIGHT}}` twice, nothing else, no DRAFT), `apps/site/dist` rebuilt clean. |
| F93 | High | Legal texts / site | **The privacy policy promised "a published open-source core so that anyone can verify the claims in this policy" while the terms said "Inborn is not open source" and the repo is private.** `support.html` promised a public issue tracker "from the 1.0 release" and `proof.html` claimed the core is MIT open source. `verification.md` §32 forbids all of it. | **Fixed.** All four texts now say what `verification.md` allows; `NOTICE.json`'s `coreLicense` carries the real `Inborn Source-Available License 1.0` and the generated licenses page no longer says open source. |
| F94 | High | Privacy policy §6/§10 | **The policy said the SQLCipher key lives in the Secure Enclave / Windows Hello-DPAPI and called the keys "hardware-backed".** A grep over `apps/mobile/src`, `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src`, `packages/core/src` returns zero hits for `SecureEnclave`, `kSecAttrTokenID`, `StrongBox`. The code uses `SecureStore` with `WHEN_UNLOCKED_THIS_DEVICE_ONLY` and the OS keychain on desktop. | **Fixed.** §6 names the iOS Keychain with its accessibility attribute, the Android Keystore, and the macOS Keychain / Windows Credential Manager; "hardware-backed keys" is out of §10. The guard re-runs the grep, so re-introducing the claim fails the suite. |
| F95 | Medium | Documents / incognito | **A file attached inside an incognito chat was copied into the document library for the session**, contradicting "never written to disk", and a crash left the copy there permanently: no sweep on launch. The index and vectors were correctly RAM-only (F69–F71); only the file was not. | **Fixed.** `copyIntoLibrary(..., { incognito })` puts it in `Paths.cache/incognito/` instead, `endSession` deletes it as before, and `DocumentLibrary.boot()` sweeps that directory on every launch. The policy line now says: held for the session, never added to the library, deleted at the end, swept on the next launch. Proof: `F95-red.txt`, then green. |
| F96 | High | Test coverage | **The F51 guard asserted on `packages/i18n/locales/en.json` only**, so the four legal Markdown texts — the ones that actually ship — had no guard at all. That is how F92 and F93 survived two review rounds. | **Fixed.** `apps/mobile/test/legal-texts.test.ts`, 38 tests over every `docs/legal` markdown, every site page, the generated `apps/site/dist`, the exact body `legalBody()` gives the screen, and the source trees behind the key claim. 18 of them fail against the texts on `main` (`F92-F94-red.txt`). |

### Found by the vc18 release pass on the OnePlus 6T (22.9.2026)

Round 31 filled the legal texts and guarded them, and `vc18` is the first Android build to carry that work. The L1
row of §U of `docs/qa/purchases-run-2026-09-11.md` read the whole of both legal screens off the phone rather than
off the file, which is what turned this up. Evidence: `docs/qa/android-vc18/`.

| # | Sev | Item | What | Fixed? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F97 | High | Legal screen (Terms) | **The Terms screen on the phone names no licensor, no phone number and no effective date — and then tells the reader to look at the effective date "at the top".** `legalBody()` (`apps/mobile/src/screens/Legal/legalBody.ts`) returns the file from its **first `##` heading**, so everything above it is cut. In `terms.md` that block is the whole identity: `Licensor: Cohen Apps ("we", "us")` and `Contact: support@inbornapp.com or +1-440-847-8502`. Read off the device, the Terms screen has **1,444 words, 0 occurrences of "Cohen Apps" and 0 of "+1-440-847-8502"**; its single `support@inbornapp.com` is incidental, inside §13's sentence about writing before a formal claim. The privacy policy escapes only because it repeats the same facts in its own §12 Contact section, which is below the first heading — the Privacy screen does show all three. The same cut removes "Effective date: 22 September 2026" from **both** screens, while the rendered §14 still says "the effective date at the top changes when we do". F96's guard is green throughout: its file-level assertions read `terms.md`, where both values are present, and the one assertion on the rendered body (`legalBody(...)` contains `support@inbornapp.com`) passes on the incidental occurrence. A guard that checks the file and a screen that renders a slice of it will disagree exactly here. | **Fixed** (round 32, branch `fixes-r27`). `legalBody.ts` no longer throws the header block away: `legalScreen()` returns it as `meta` — the `Label: value` lines above the first `##`, minus the `Spec basis` edit note the screen already shows and any `Status` draft banner — and `Legal.tsx` renders those lines as a small block under the title, parsed from the markdown, nothing hardcoded. Terms now shows `Effective date: 22 September 2026`, `Licensor: Cohen Apps ("we", "us")` and `Contact: support@inbornapp.com or +1-440-847-8502…`; Privacy gains the effective date it was also missing. The F96 guard is extended to the **rendered** text of each screen: eight new assertions over `[...meta, body]` for `Cohen Apps`, the email, the phone and the effective date, that the identity comes from the block itself and not from a section that repeats it, that `Legal.tsx` hardcodes none of the values, and a parser case over a synthetic document. Watched red first: `docs/qa/fixes-r27/red.txt` (8 failed / 40 passed with main's rendering restored — the file-level assertions stay green throughout, which is the point). Green: `green.txt`, 48/48. Counts after: `docs/qa/fixes-r27/f97-rendered-after.txt`, the same measurement as `android-vc18/f97-legalbody-proof.txt` — terms `Cohen Apps` rendered 0→1, `+1-440-847-8502` 0→1, `Effective date` 0→1. **Re-read on the phone by the `vc19` pass** (§V of `docs/qa/purchases-run-2026-09-11.md`): on versionCode 19, delivered by Google Play to the OnePlus 6T, the Terms screen's head carries `Effective date: 22 September 2026`, `Licensor: Cohen Apps ("we", "us")` and the `+1-440-847-8502` contact line — `docs/qa/android-vc19/l1-01-legal-terms-identity.png`, whole screen in `legal-terms-on-device.txt` (52 lines, 1,600 words, 0 `{{`). |

| # | Sev | Item | What | Fixed? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F98 | Medium | Legal screen (Terms), same defect as F97 (fixed in round 32) | **The in-app Terms screen names no licensor and gives no phone number, in the build shipped to fix exactly that.** `legalBody()` returns the source from its first `## ` heading, dropping everything above it. `privacy-policy.md` survives that cut because it repeats the identity in a numbered section (line 97 `Cohen Apps`, line 99 the email and `+1-440-847-8502`), but `terms.md` carries `Licensor: Cohen Apps ("we", "us")`, `Effective date: 22 September 2026`, the `+1-440-847-8502` contact line **and** the scope paragraph ("These terms apply to the Inborn apps for iOS, Android, Windows and macOS…") **only** in that head block. Counted on the rendered body: `Cohen Apps` privacy file 2 / screen 1, terms file 1 / **screen 0**; `+1-440-847-8502` privacy 1 / 1, terms 1 / **screen 0**. The phone confirms it — `r-19b-legal-terms.png` opens at "1. License" under the header, with no identity block above it. The date is not lost (the screen prints `LAST EDITED 22 September 2026` from its own regex over the raw doc), but the licensor and the phone are. | **Fixed (round 32, branch `fixes-r27`, the same change that closed F97) and re-read on the phone in TestFlight 1.0.0 (15).** `legalScreen()` now returns the head block as `meta` and `Legal.tsx` renders it under the title. The Terms screen on the device opens on `Effective date: 22 September 2026`, `Licensor: Cohen Apps ("we", "us")` and `Contact: support@inbornapp.com or +1-440-847-8502. We have no physical reception and offer no in-person service.`, above "1. License" — against 0 occurrences of both `Cohen Apps` and `+1-440-847-8502` when this was filed. Proof off the phone: `docs/qa/ios-device-pass-15/r-19b-legal-terms.png`, row 2 of `docs/qa/ios-device-pass-15-2026-09-23.md`; and in the shipped Hermes bundle `docs/qa/ios-build-15-2026-09-23.md`. Why round 31's suite missed it: `apps/mobile/test/legal-texts.test.ts` asserts `Cohen Apps` and `+1-440-847-8502` on **`read("docs/legal/terms.md")`**, the file, while the only assertion it makes on `legalBody(...)`, what the screen shows, is no `{{`, no draft notice and `support@inbornapp.com`. That is the same shape as F96: a guard one layer away from the reader. The complement was run to prove the check is not vacuous — `expect(legalBody(terms)).toContain("Cohen Apps")` **fails** today and the identical assertion on the privacy policy **passes** (`scratchpad/ios-build-14/F98-terms-identity.txt`). Either repeat the licensor and contact inside a `##` section of `terms.md` as the privacy policy already does, or have `Legal.tsx` render the head block; then extend the guard to assert every identity value on `legalBody()`, not on the file. |

### Found by the MosheAI store-copy read (verdict 7, 23.9.2026)

MosheAI read the eight submission-ready listings in `docs/store/` against the legal texts round 31 and round 32 had just
corrected, and found the store copy still making the claims those texts had stopped making. Evidence: `docs/qa/store-copy/`.

| # | Sev | Item | What | Fixed? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F99 | High | Store listings, all 8 locales (`docs/store/listing.*.json`) | **The store copy still claimed an open-source core, a published per-release hash, and an incognito mode that never touches disk — three claims the app's own legal texts had already been corrected to stop making — and offered a DeepSeek keyword for a model the catalogue does not contain.** `docs/legal/verification.md` lists "open source", "published core" and "reproducible build" under wording that may never be published while the repository is private, `terms.md` §1 says in so many words that Inborn is not open source, and `privacy-policy.md` §10 says the source is not public. The listings said the opposite in every language: `Open Source` (de), `open source` (en, fr), `código abierto` (es), `código aberto` (pt-BR), `オープンソース` (ja), `오픈소스` (ko), `開放原始碼` (zh-Hant), each with a published-hash promise beside it (`veröffentlichtem Hash`, `empreinte publiée`, `hash publicado`, `ハッシュを公開`, `해시를 공개`, `公布雜湊值`) that no reproducible build can back. The disk claim was false in a second way: `privacy-policy.md` §6 says an incognito **attachment** is held in a temporary file for the length of the session and deleted at its end, so "never touches disk" / "never writes to disk" / `nie auf die Platte schreibt` / `n'écrit jamais sur le disque` / `nunca escribe en el disco` / `nunca grava no disco` / `ディスクに一切書き込まない` / `디스크에 한 글자도 쓰지 않는` / `完全不寫入磁碟` overstates it in all eight. `deepseek` sat in the Apple keyword field of en, ko and zh-Hant; the only `deepseek` in the codebase is `deepseek2` in the GGUF architecture allow-list (`packages/core/src/catalog/gguf.ts:210`), an import format, not a model we ship or list. The checker that was supposed to catch this, `docs/store/scripts/check-store-copy.mjs`, only measured field lengths and keyword hygiene, and it was not wired into any gate. | **Fixed** (round 33, branch `store-copy`). 35 lines changed across the eight listings, nothing else moved (`docs/qa/store-copy/copy-diff.txt`). The source claim is replaced by what `verification.md` says is safe to publish — every check in the bullet list above it happens outside the app with no cooperation from us, and the Proof screen names the exact build — using each locale's shipped name for that screen (`Nachweis`, `Preuve`, `Prueba`, `Prova`, `証明`, `증명`, `證明`, from `packages/i18n/locales/*.json`). The incognito claim becomes "saves nothing and ends with the session", which is what §6 actually says. `deepseek` is removed from all three keyword fields. `check-store-copy.mjs` gains five banned-claim rules (open-source, disk, published-hash, DeepSeek, and a second disk rule for Korean and Traditional Chinese) that walk **every** string in a listing, not a field list, so a banned phrase in `promotional_text`, `whats_new`, a screenshot line, the reviewer notes or an A/B variant fails too. It is wired into `pn test` as `check:store`, ahead of every test runner. Watched red first: `docs/qa/store-copy/red.txt` — **51 errors over the eight listings exactly as they stood on `main` 202db50**, every rule firing in every language it was written for, plus `pn test` exiting 1 at `check:store` with one banned phrase put back. `green.txt` is clean and every field is still within its store limit. **Not done:** nothing was pushed to App Store Connect — the repository has no script that writes store metadata and the brief said not to build one; see `docs/qa/store-copy/asc-update.txt`. |

### Found by the model-switch stream (round 40, branch `model-switch`, 23.9.2026)

Moshe, 23.9.2026: *"How do I change model? Is there a choice of other models? I did not find how to switch models, and how do
you recommend models: by language and by the action I ask for, no? Apart from Instant I saw no other models."* And on Hebrew:
*"Why does Hebrew come out as gibberish 'on purpose'? Do not deal with Hebrew specifically; if a user wants Hebrew let him, we
don't push it but we don't block it."* Evidence: `docs/qa/model-switch/` (`before/`, `after/`, taken by `shots.mjs` against the
real web export at 390 and 1440).

| # | Sev | Item | What | Fixed? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F150 | High | Chat header, every platform (`apps/mobile/src/screens/Chat.tsx`) | **There was no way to switch model from the chat, and nothing in the chat ever named a second model.** The header's `model-chip` carried the model's name and the `model-picker` shortcut was bound to it, but both opened `ChatSettingsSheet`, where the model is a `<View>` chip that is not pressable and not a control at all — name, persona, system prompt, thinking switch, and no door to another model. Proof of the state Moshe met: `before/02-390-model-chip-opens.png` and `before/02-1440-model-chip-opens.png` — "Chat settings", `MODEL` / `INSTANT` as a dead chip. The recommendation engine (`packages/core/src/catalog/recommend.ts`, round 11) and the fit map were already shipped, but only the vault at `/vault` read them, three taps away behind the drawer, so a user who never opened the vault saw exactly one model for the life of the app. | **Fixed.** The chip and the `model-picker` shortcut now open a new Model sheet (`apps/mobile/src/components/chat/ModelSheet.tsx` + `ChatModelSheet.tsx`, spec §8.4 S30b): the §7.8 recommendation line at the top, then `On this device` (installed, ranked, the loaded one marked `In use`, the rest one tap to switch), `Fits your phone` (downloadable, each row with what it is good at from the §6.1 fit map, the language tier for this chat, the size, and a Download button that expands the §5.1 confirmation with the Wi-Fi-only switch), and `Too big for N GB` greyed with the reason. Chat settings and the full vault stay reachable from its footer. The ordering and the sections come from one new pure function, `modelChoices`, built on the existing `rankModels` — no second opinion. After: `after/02-390-model-chip-opens.png`, `after/02-1440-model-chip-opens.png`, `after/06-*-model-sheet-footer.png`. Tests: `packages/core/test/catalog-model-choices.test.ts` (13 cases), `apps/mobile/src/components/chat/modelSheet.test.ts` (21, and the chip assertion was watched red by putting `setSettingsOpen` back). |
| F151 | Medium | Weak-language notice (`Chat.tsx`, `chat.modelWeak`) | **The notice told the user the loaded model was weak in their language and then stopped**: `INSTANT is weak in Hebrew`, a grey line with no model named and nothing to tap (`before/03-390-hebrew-answer.png`). The advice card that does name a better model only fires when the model is weak *and* the ranking finds a model better on that dimension without being worse on the other, and it is snoozed per chat after one showing — so the common case (Hebrew on Instant, card already dismissed) left the user with a complaint and no way to act on it. | **Fixed.** The line now names the model that does better and carries the action: `INSTANT is weak in Hebrew. SHARP handles it better.` + `Switch to SHARP` when it is installed, else it opens the Model sheet on that model. It reads a new core helper, `betterForLanguage`, which weighs the language alone (a language the model cannot write is reason enough; the task only breaks the tie between the models that fix it) and prefers an installed model, naming the best installable one as `best`. It stands down when the advice card is already making the same point, and on the browser tier, which cannot switch, where the honest `NOTHING ON THIS BROWSER IS GOOD AT CHAT IN HEBREW · CLOSEST: INSTANT` line already says it (`after/03-390-hebrew-answer.png`). Tests: `catalog-model-choices.test.ts` `betterForLanguage` block, `modelSheet.test.ts` last block. |
| F152 | Medium | Answer length, Hebrew (`packages/core/src/chat/length.ts`) | **An explicit length asked in Hebrew was not heard, while the same ask in English was.** `detectExplicitLength` reads number-then-unit (`in one sentence`, `100단어로`, `ב-120 מילים`) and its unit table already carried the Hebrew words, but Hebrew writes the small numbers *after* the unit and spells them — `ענה במשפט אחד` is "answer in sentence one". So `planAnswerLength("מה בירת צרפת? ענה במשפט אחד")` returned `explicit: false, maxTokens: 224` against `explicit: true, words: 25, maxTokens: 160` for the identical English sentence. Found by the pass-through test written for Moshe's question, not by a report; it is exactly the quiet kind of "Hebrew is treated worse" he was asking about. | **Fixed.** A Hebrew table beside the existing ones, reading both orders (`שלושה משפטים` and `משפט אחד`) and its spelled numbers, with a letter lookahead so `אחדות` is not read as `אחד`. `detectExplicitLength("ענה במשפט אחד")` now equals `detectExplicitLength("answer in one sentence")`. Visible in `after/03-390-hebrew-answer.png`: one sentence, against the two-clause answer in `before/`. Tests: two cases in `packages/core/test/fixes-r19.test.ts`, including the complement (a Hebrew unit with no number, and `משפט אחדות`, are not lengths). |
| F153 | Low | Model sheet on the browser and desktop tiers (§14.3) | **The first cut of the sheet told a browser user that a model it can never load is "RECOMMENDED ON THIS BROWSER".** The browser tier holds one model in OPFS and has no vault, so ranking over the whole catalogue produced `RECOMMENDED ON THIS BROWSER · CHAT IN ENGLISH` with the tag sitting on `SHARP`, under a paragraph explaining that Sharp lives in the app. Caught in the browser before the branch was pushed, not by a test. | **Fixed.** `modelChoices` takes `recommendAmong`, the ids this tier can actually load; the managed tier passes its installed list, so the line names the model in use and falls back to the honest `NOTHING ON THIS BROWSER IS GOOD AT CHAT IN HEBREW · CLOSEST: INSTANT` when that one is weak. The second section is headed `In the app` there instead of `Fits your browser`, and its rows carry no action. `after/02-1440-model-chip-opens.png`, `after/05-390-model-sheet-hebrew.png`. Test: the `recommendAmong` case in `catalog-model-choices.test.ts`, which also asserts the unrestricted input still recommends `fast`. |
| F154 | — | Hebrew is not degraded anywhere (verification, not a defect) | Moshe asked whether Hebrew is made worse on purpose. Swept `packages/core/src/chat` and `apps/mobile/src` for `he`, `hebrew`, `rtl`: the only language-aware code is `directionOf`/`scriptOf` (bidi rendering), `languageHint` (adds `The user writes in Hebrew. Answer in Hebrew.` to the system prompt), `detectLanguage`, the token estimate (2 chars per token for Hebrew/Arabic/Cyrillic, a budget figure, not a cap on the text), the length table above, and the family-safe phrase list, which carries Hebrew at exactly the same level as the other eight languages and only as explicit phrases. **No path blocks, rewrites, transliterates or shortens a Hebrew turn.** | **Proven, and guarded.** `packages/core/test/chat-hebrew-unchanged.test.ts` replays the chat's real send path (`screenText` → `planAnswerLength` → `composeSystemPrompt`+`languageHint` → `buildPrompt`) on a Hebrew turn: the user's text is the last message code point for code point, niqqud, geresh, maqaf and an embedded RLM survive, the system prompt differs from the English one by the hint block and nothing else, and family-safe refuses none of five ordinary Hebrew turns in either setting. The complement is asserted too, so the guard cannot pass by being empty: the one explicit Hebrew phrase the safety list ships still fires, and the same sentence with an ordinary noun does not. What is left is the models themselves — Instant and Fast rate Hebrew `none`, Sharp and Phi `basic` — which the sheet now states on every row instead of hiding (`after/05-390-model-sheet-hebrew.png`). |
### Found while making the legal texts come from the site (round 42, branch `legal-from-site`, 23.9.2026)

Moshe: "The privacy policy and terms of use in the app MUST come from the site so we can update them; it must not be
local on the phone, very important. Also the site needs an accessibility policy." Evidence: `docs/qa/legal-from-site/`.

| # | Sev | Item | What | Fixed? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F155 | High | In-app legal screens, all four documents | **Every legal text a user or a reviewer reads in the app was frozen at build time, with nothing on the screen saying so and no way to reach the version we can actually update.** `Legal.tsx` imported `privacy-policy.md` and `terms.md` straight into the bundle, `Licenses.tsx` imported `NOTICE.json`, and each screen rendered them as the document, not as a copy of one. Correcting a policy therefore meant shipping a store build: the text a regulator or an App Store reviewer opens could not be changed for as long as review takes. The obvious fix — fetch it — is the one thing this product cannot do: the Android release manifest declares no `INTERNET` permission at all (decision D3, `docs/legal/app-privacy-details.md` §4.2), so a fetch would either fail on Android or force the permission that the whole proof rests on. There was also no accessibility statement anywhere in the repository, and the site's footer and the store questionnaires had no field for one. | **Fixed.** The site is the source and `https://inbornapp.com/<doc>` is the canonical URL (`apps/mobile/src/lib/legalLinks.ts`, one map for all four documents). Every legal screen now opens with a block that labels what you are reading — `OFFLINE COPY · EFFECTIVE 23 September 2026`, taken from the document's own `Effective date:` line, and `OFFLINE COPY · INVENTORY 2026-09-05` from `NOTICE.json` on the licenses screen — a primary button "Read the current version at inbornapp.com/<doc>", and one line saying the website version is the one that applies. The button is `Linking.openURL`, which hands the URL to the system browser and needs no network permission, so this works unchanged on Android; a guard asserts no legal screen contains `fetch(` or `XMLHttpRequest`. The new accessibility statement is the fourth document and reaches the app the same way, from About. Proof: `docs/qa/legal-from-site/app-legal-{accessibility,privacy,terms}-390.png`, `app-legal-{accessibility,privacy}-1440.png`, `app-licenses-{390,1440}.png`. Guards watched red first: with the `/accessibility` page deleted from the site build, "every path the app opens is a page the site build produces" fails naming the missing route; with the block removed from `Legal.tsx`, "the screen that shows a document is the screen that offers its live page" fails (`docs/qa/legal-from-site/guards-fail.md`). The first version of that second guard passed the sabotage because it matched the import line, so it was tightened to the rendered element. |
| F156 | Medium | `apps/site/src/site.css`, dark scheme | **The website's tertiary text failed WCAG AA on every dark surface, and the accessibility statement was about to claim the opposite.** The site hard-copies the palette instead of importing `packages/ui/src/tokens.ts`, and `--text-3` had drifted to `#667380` against the app's tested `#7A8794`: **4.01:1 on `--bg`, 3.90 on `--well`, 3.74 on `--surface-1`, 3.49 on `--surface-2`**, all under 4.5:1, while the app's value gives 4.62–5.30. It carries real text — table headers, mono labels, price sub-labels, badges, platform tags, most of it 11 px uppercase mono. The light scheme had drifted too (`#5C6873` vs `#5E6975`), passing but unwatched. Nothing tested the site's palette: `packages/ui/src/tokens.test.ts` reads the tokens, which the site never imports. Found by the security-engineer review of the accessibility draft, which is what the review was for. | **Fixed.** Both schemes' `--text-3` now equal the app's tokens, and a new guard reads `site.css` itself, maps all thirteen colour variables to their token and asserts both equality **and** the 4.5:1 ratio for the three ink colours on all four surfaces, so a future drift fails whether or not it happens to still pass. Watched red: reverting `--text-3` to `#667380` fails two tests, `dark --text-3: expected '#667380' to be '#7A8794'` and `dark --text-3 on --bg: expected 4.013604452184076 to be greater than or equal to 4.5`. Before/after on the page, with the computed colour read out of the browser: `site-licenses-1440-dark-BEFORE-contrast.png` (`th` colour `rgb(102, 115, 128)`) and `site-licenses-1440-dark-AFTER-contrast.png` (`rgb(122, 135, 148)`), both on `rgb(10, 13, 17)`. Note for whoever screenshots this site next: `--force-prefers-color-scheme` is ignored by the headless shell, so the dark scheme needs Playwright's `colorScheme: "dark"` context. |
| F157 | Medium | `packages/ui/src/tokens.ts`, `dark.onDanger` on `dark.danger` | **The delete and wipe buttons' text is 3.10:1 in the dark theme, and the contrast test does not cover it.** `onDanger` `#F3F5F7` on `danger` `#F25555` gives 3.10:1; in the light theme `danger` text on `well` gives 4.30:1. Both are under AA for normal text. `tokens.test.ts` asserts only `text`/`text2`/`text3` on the four surfaces plus `ctaText` on `ctaFill`, so `accent`, `sealed` and `danger` are untested — which is how this survived. Also found by the security-engineer review; it is the first thing an auditor with a contrast checker finds. | **Not fixed here, declared instead.** Changing a semantic brand colour is a design decision that belongs to whoever owns the palette, not to a legal-text round, and inventing a value here would collide with the design streams running in parallel. So the accessibility statement's §5 states the number in public — "Their text sits at about **3.1:1** on the red fill" — and a guard pins it: `contrastRatio(dark.onDanger, dark.danger)` must still be under 4.5 and close to 3.1, and the statement must still contain that sentence. Watched red: setting `onDanger` to `#3A0000` (5.26:1) fails with `expected 5.2597522387438005 to be less than 4.5`, so the day the colour is fixed the test forces the gap out of the published statement, which is what §7 of that statement promises. **Open for the palette owner:** pick a `danger`/`onDanger` pair that reaches 4.5:1 in both themes, extend `tokens.test.ts` to cover `danger`, `accent` and `sealed`, then delete the bullet from `docs/legal/accessibility-policy.md` §5 in the same change. |
### Found by Moshe in the browser and on the phone (premium entry, 23.9.2026)

Moshe: "buttons for premium, to see what's in premium, and tapping things that lead to premium according to what we
decided: this needs adding so users know there is premium." · "the other attach buttons show they are disabled, but why
doesn't tapping them take me to the paywall?" · "`http://127.0.0.1:8477/paywall` is very strange: only 'Pay once. Own
it… Pro is sold in the iOS, Android, Windows and macOS apps…'. Where are the prices? Very naked."
Reproduced and re-proved in the browser at 390 and 1440. Evidence: `docs/qa/premium-entry/`.

| # | Sev | Item | What | Fixed? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F145 | High | Every gated tap, all screens (`grep -rn '"/paywall"' apps/mobile/src` on `main` 73c7e18) | **Every refusal opened the same generic price list.** All fourteen doors — the mic, the strict switch, folders, memory, "remember this", export-all, the persona limit, a Pro-only model, the Work redaction sheet, the office-format card, the OCR button, the detailed ledger, the share target and the `/voice` deep link — pushed a bare `router.push("/paywall")`. The person who had just been refused one specific thing met "Pay once. Own it." and had to work out for themselves which of the two prices, if either, lifted what they tried. The value-moment mechanism (`paywallFor`) already computed the exact reason and then threw it away at the door. | **Fixed** (round 39, branch `premium-entry`). `reasonOf(moment)` and `PAYWALL_REASONS` extend `packages/core/src/license/moments.ts` — the existing mechanism, not a second one: a feature moment carries its own feature id, the others carry their kind. One door, `openPaywall(reason)` (`apps/mobile/src/license/openPaywall.ts`), puts it on the route; `app/paywall.tsx` reads it back through `readReason()` (anything not on the list is ignored) and S60 opens with `paywall.why.<reason>` in the accent rule above the cards. Every call site now names its reason. Proved end to end in the browser, not only in a test: tapping the PRO tag on the strict switch lands on `/paywall?reason=strictDocuments` and the screen reads "You asked for answers only from your documents. Pro turns that on." (`after-07-attach-sheet-390.png` → `after-08-strict-tap-to-paywall-390.png`; `after-04-why-document-*.png` for the `document` reason). Guards: `apps/mobile/test/premium-entry.test.ts` fails on any `push("/paywall")` outside the door, on any `onOpenPaywall?.()` / `onUnlock?.()` with no reason, and on a reason S60 does not know; `packages/core/test/license-compare.test.ts` fails when a reason has no `paywall.why.*` line in `en.json` or names a capability this build does not ship. Copy in all 9 locales. |
| F146 | High | Attach sheet, document rows (`apps/mobile/src/screens/Chat.tsx`, `components/chat/AttachSheet.tsx`) | **A Free user could tick a second, third and fourth document into a chat for nothing.** §7.3 row 1 gives Free one file per chat, and both other doors into the library enforce it — the picker through `pickIntoLibrary` and the share target through `fileIntake`. The sheet's own list did not: `onAttach={docs.attach}` went straight to `library.attach(key, docId)` with no tier question anywhere on the path. The row also gave no sign it was gated, which is the other half of what Moshe asked about: a control that looks live and silently walks past the limit. | **Fixed** (round 39, branch `premium-entry`). Chat computes `attachLocked = paywallFor(tier, { kind: "document", existing: docs.documents.length })` — the same moment the picker asks — and hands it to the sheet. A locked row stays tappable, wears the PRO tag, carries `quick.filePro` ("Free keeps one file at a time. Remove it to add another, or get Pro for a library.") as its hint, and opens S60 with reason `document`. Detaching an already-attached file is never gated (removing your own data is not sold, §7.5). The Free photos-per-message cap now also says what happened (`chat.attach.photoLimit`) before it opens S60 with reason `photos`, instead of a silent push. Guard: `premium-entry.test.ts` F146 block, watched red — restoring `onAttach={docs.attach}` fails it. |
| F147 | Medium | Settings (`screens/Settings/Settings.tsx`), chat header menu (`components/chat/ChatSettingsSheet.tsx`) | **Nothing in the app said Pro existed until something was refused.** `grep -rn "paywall" apps/mobile/src/screens/Settings` on `main` returned nothing: there was no row, no badge and no link anywhere a person could go on purpose to find out what they are on or what they would get. The only way to S60 was to walk into a wall. | **Fixed** (round 39, branch `premium-entry`). Settings opens with an "INBORN PRO" section: "See what's in Pro", a sub-line per tier (`paywall.entry.sub.free|pro|work`), the current tier as the row value (FREE / PRO / WORK) and a chevron to S60 — `after-05-settings-pro-row-390.png`. The sheet the chat header opens carries the same tier chip and link at the top — after round 40 that is the model sheet, and Chat settings keeps one too — `after-06-tier-chip-1440.png`. Both open S60 with no reason, because nothing was refused. |
| F148 | Medium | S60 paywall (`screens/paywall/PaywallScreen.tsx`) | **The paywall priced two tiers and never said what separates them.** Each card showed five bullets for the tier it sells; there was no place that put Free, Pro and Work beside each other, so "what's in premium" could not be answered without buying. | **Fixed** (round 39, branch `premium-entry`). `COMPARE_ROWS` / `compareCell()` (`packages/core/src/license/compare.ts`) hold the §7.3 matrix in one machine-readable place; `CompareTable.tsx` renders 18 rows × Free / Pro / Work and marks the column the person is on. Every cell is computed from the same `can()` / `limits()` the screens ask, so the table cannot promise what the gates refuse — the three ungated rows read "included" everywhere (Free is a complete product, §12.3), the limits read 1 / ∞ / ∞ and 3 / ∞ / ∞, and the five Work-only rows are the ones Pro does not have. `after-03-compare-table-390.png`, `after-03-compare-table-1440.png`. Guards in `license-compare.test.ts`, watched red: adding `professionPacks` (on `UNBUILT_FEATURES`) as a row fails three of them. |
| F149 | High | Web paywall `/paywall` (`screens/paywall/WebStoreBlock.tsx`, `apps/web/build.mjs`, `scripts/serve-web.mjs`) | **In a browser the paywall named no price at all, and the URL did not resolve.** With `store === null` the screen rendered one sentence — `paywall.noStore`, "Pro is sold in the iOS, Android, Windows and macOS apps. The browser version stays free." — and nothing else: no price, no tiers, no way to the app. Exactly what Moshe saw (`before-02-web-paywall-390.png`, `before-02-web-paywall-1440.png`, built from `main` 73c7e18). Separately, `/paywall` was a 404 on a static host: the export is one `index.html` and neither the deployable build nor the dev server had a rewrite, so a shared link to any route but `/` would have 404'd on Cloudflare Pages too. | **Fixed** (round 39, branch `premium-entry`). `WebStoreBlock` replaces the bare line with both tiers priced from the catalogue (`offersFor("free", …)` + `fallbackPrice`, never a literal in the page), their value lines, "Prices are US list prices. Your store shows the price where you are.", three buttons to App Store / Google Play / Desktop (`STORE_LINKS` in `apps/mobile/src/web/links.ts`, each pointing at `inbornapp.com/download` until that listing is live), the promise that the browser stays free, and the comparison table below. `after-02-web-paywall-390.png`, `after-02-web-paywall-1440.png`. The route resolves because the build now writes `_redirects` (`/* /index.html 200`) and `scripts/serve-web.mjs` falls back to `index.html` for any extensionless path — the same one-document rule in both places. `pn web:smoke` stays green (first visit 18.9 s · 25 tok/s, offline visit 1.7 s · 0 model fetches, onboarding walked, vault / phone / no-space doors). |
### Found by Moshe on the onboarding model step (23.9.2026)

Moshe walked the first run in the browser and stopped on S02 and S03: an offer the screen could not start, a switch that
governs nothing on most platforms, and a request to turn off his network before he had seen a single answer. Evidence:
`docs/qa/onboarding-rework/` (`before/` and `after/` at 390 / 768 / 1440 in both schemes, `layout-two-options/` for the
two-option layout the browser cannot reach on its own, `shots.mjs` the driver, `web-smoke.txt` the gate run).

| # | Sev | Item | What | Fixed? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F120 | High | S02 model step (`apps/mobile/src/screens/Onboarding/ModelChoice.tsx`) | **The step offered a "Fast (1.2 GB)" download it had no way to start.** The offer card carried a size, a sentence and a Wi-Fi switch, and no action at all: both footer buttons, `start-chatting` and `instantOnly`, ran the same `router.push("/onboarding/airplane")`. On the web, where Moshe read it, the vault cannot deliver anything (`HttpsDelivery.plan` returns null for `Platform.OS === "web"`), so the card was dead by construction; `before/onboarding-model-390-dark.png`. | **Fixed** (round 36, branch `onboarding-rework`). The step is now a choice between the models this platform can actually deliver, computed by one pure function (`apps/mobile/src/screens/Onboarding/modelStep.ts`): a model is an option when it is already on the device or when `delivery.plan()` is non-null, and it is dropped otherwise. Choosing one that needs delivery turns the primary button into `Download {name} · {size}` and starts `vault.install(id)` on the spot. 13 unit tests (`modelStep.test.ts`) cover iOS, Android before and after the pack lands, the browser, the RAM floor, the Pro tier, no disk space, a download in flight. `after/onboarding-model-*.png`, `layout-two-options/onboarding-model-model-option-fast-*.png`. |
| F121 | Medium | S02 Wi-Fi switch | **"Wi-Fi only" was shown to every platform and changed the behaviour of exactly one.** `wifiOnly` is read only by `HttpsDelivery` (`shouldWait`, `resume.ts:55`); `PlayDelivery` never receives the preference at all, because Play asks for cellular consent itself, and the browser has no download to govern and cannot tell a metered connection from a wired one. Moshe: "it should also fit desktops on a wired connection". | **Fixed.** The switch appears only when an option on this screen would download over HTTPS, which today means iOS, and it moved out of the model card into a block of its own with a line saying what it does (`onboarding.model.wifiOnly`, `onboarding.model.wifiOnlyHint`). Android gets the true sentence instead: "Google Play delivers the model, because Inborn itself has no internet permission." The browser and desktop get neither. Asserted in `modelStep.test.ts` for all three platforms. |
| F122 | Medium | S03 airplane test, onboarding chain | **The onboarding asked the user to turn off their network, in step 3 of 5, without saying why.** The screen opened on "Prove it to yourself." followed straight by "1. Turn on Airplane Mode.", with no reason and no visual break from the model step before it. Moshe: "here I don't even know why you want me in airplane mode"; `before/airplane-test-390-dark.png`. | **Fixed.** The airplane test left the onboarding chain (welcome to model to sealed to lock) and lives on S50 Proof, the screen whose job is proving, under an AIRPLANE TEST section whose first line is the reason: "Turn on Airplane Mode and ask anything. The answer still comes, because it was only ever coming from this {device}." The same line now opens the test screen itself, above the steps, and each numbered step sits in its own card. `after/proof-*.png`, `after/airplane-test-*.png`. Matches Apple's onboarding guidance (ask where the function is used, not up front) and the 28 % higher grant rate reported for deferred permission asks. |
| F123 | Medium | S02 offer timing | **The choice arrived after the download.** Moshe: "you offer optional Fast after I already downloaded one pack; why not let me choose before?" The old step presented Instant as a finished fact and Fast as an afterthought below it, so the only real decision the user had was made for them. | **Fixed.** Both models are presented as siblings before anything moves, each with its size, where it comes from, what it is good at and which languages it is strong in, and the bigger one carries a `Better in {language}` badge when the catalogue rates it above the small one in the reader's own language. The screen opens on the model that already works, so the recommendation never spends a gigabyte by itself. |
| F124 | Low | S02 source line on the web | **"Instant · built in" was false in the browser**, where the model is downloaded into OPFS by the download door before onboarding ever runs, and the size line printed a locale-typed "0.5 GB". | **Fixed.** Every source line is read off the delivery, never guessed: `inside the app` (bundled), `delivered by Google Play`, `Google Play is delivering it now`, `one download from {host}`, `already on this {device}`. The size comes from the catalogue in all of them, and the QA F24 guard in `packages/i18n/test/locales.test.ts` now walks all six sized keys in all nine locale files instead of the one key it used to watch (watched red first: a hand-typed `1,2 GB` in `de.json` fails it). |
| F124b | Low | S04 Sealed (`apps/mobile/src/screens/Onboarding/Sealed.tsx`) | **Taking the airplane test out of the onboarding chain (F122) left a first-run user who never opens Proof with no way to meet the ritual at all.** Raised as a judgement call with the change, not found in use. | **Fixed** (lead's decision, same round). The seal screen offers it as a secondary link under Start, with the reason inside the label rather than above it: `Prove it: turn on Airplane Mode and ask` (`onboarding.sealed.prove`, all eight locales plus `pseudo`). It routes to `/proof/airplane`, the same screen the Proof page opens, and is disabled until the seal has closed, so it never competes with Start. Proven live in the browser, not only on the source: `after/onboarding-sealed-390-dark.png` and `after/onboarding-sealed-1440-light.png` for the screen, `after/onboarding-sealed-sealed-prove-*.png` for where the tap lands. `apps/mobile/src/screens/Onboarding/sealed.test.ts` asserts the wiring and the label in every locale, watched red twice: pointing the link at `/proof` and demoting it from `variant="link"` each fail it. |


### Found by Moshe in the browser (web build, 23.9.2026)

Moshe tested `apps/web` (the RN-web bundle the Tauri desktop also runs): "ATTACH DOCUMENTS" closes the sheet and no
file dialog opens; popups that vanish without doing anything happened several more times; the Wi-Fi-only switch does
not say which side is on; "New chat" in the side bar wraps to two lines. Reproduced headlessly over the built
`apps/web/dist` before anything was touched and re-measured after. Evidence: `docs/qa/web-bugs/`.

| # | Sev | Item | What | Fixed? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F100 | High | Attach sheet → "Add a file", browser and desktop tiers (`apps/mobile/src/documents/importPicker.ts`) | **Moshe: clicking ATTACH DOCUMENTS closes the sheet and no file dialog opens.** Reproduced headlessly over `apps/web/dist`: the click fires, the sheet closes, **zero** `filechooser` events, no page error, no toast, no flash — `docs/qa/web-bugs/before-f100-result.json` and `before-f100-after-add-a-file.png`. The sheet audit scores it a dead end, the only real one of the 18 sheet items reachable in a browser (`before-sheet-audit.json`). The cause is not the sheet and not the gesture: **`expo-file-system` ships no file picker for the web at all.** Its web module is `pickFileAsync: () => { console.warn('expo-file-system is not supported on web'); return Promise.resolve(); }`, and the console in the before run carries exactly that warning. `File.pickFileAsync` then does `new File(file.uri)` on that `undefined`, wraps the whole body in `try/catch`, and reports the resulting TypeError as `{ result: null, canceled: true }` — so `pickIntoLibrary` read a user-cancelled pick, every time, and had nothing to say. The same door on the Documents screen was dead for the same reason. | **Fixed.** `apps/mobile/src/documents/importPicker.web.ts` is a browser picker of our own: an `<input type="file">` with `PICK_TYPES` as its `accept`, clicked with nothing awaited above the click so it stays inside the gesture, resolved on `change` or `cancel`, and the chosen `File` registered as a blob by the same `registerBlob` the desktop's drag-and-drop uses. It asks `paywallFor` and `fileIntake` in the same order as the phone's picker, so the Free one-file cap and the Work formats gate a browser exactly as they gate a phone; `gates-wired.test.ts` now lists it as a door, which is what forces that. After, same driver, same page: **1** `filechooser`, the picked `probe.txt` imported and attached to the composer (`after-f100-result.json`, `after-f100-after-add-a-file.png`). `pn web:smoke` green. |
| F101 | — | Every Sheet/Modal on the web build (audit, not a defect) | **Moshe: popups that vanish without doing anything happened several more times, unlisted.** The brief's two hypotheses were the backdrop `Pressable` swallowing the item's pointer event, and the `presented.key` remount. Both are wrong. Every item of every sheet a browser can reach was clicked one at a time in a fresh page — attach, chat settings, new chat, personas, memory, wipe, 18 items — and each one did something: `attach-templates` opens the templates sheet, `attach-manage` opens the documents panel, `attach-strict` and every gated memory row open the paywall, `persona-add` opens the editor, `wipe-step1` advances to step 2 (`docs/qa/web-bugs/after-sheet-audit.txt`). The one row the audit scored a dead end, `start-chat`, is a false positive of its heuristic: with the writer persona picked it does start that chat, the header changing from no persona to `Writer`. **The 320 ms hand-over is innocent too**, which is worth recording because it is the obvious suspect: a control page clicked a chooser and a download both inside the gesture and from a 320 ms timer, and all four fired, in Chromium **and** in WebKit — Chrome's transient activation lasts five seconds (`activation-control.json`, `activation-control-webkit.json`). So the answer to "several more times" is F100 and, as far as a browser can be driven, nothing else. | **No second defect found; the hand-over tidied anyway.** `afterSheetClose` moved out of `Chat.tsx` into `apps/mobile/src/lib/sheetHandover.ts` and now runs the action inside the gesture on web: its whole reason is that Android freezes when a Modal opens in the frame another one dismisses, and a browser has no Modal to collide with, so the wait there was 320 ms of nothing between the tap and the result. Three screens had copied the timer by hand — `MemorySheet`, `PersonasSheet`, `VaultScreen` — and now call the shared one, so there is one place left to get a platform rule wrong; a guard fails on any `setTimeout(…, 320)` left in `apps/mobile/src`. Watched red both ways: deleting the web branch fails `runs inside the gesture on web`, and the phones' branch is asserted at 319 ms and 320 ms. A third guard pins what the audit found by hand — the panel must be rendered after the backdrop in both `Sheet.tsx` files. |
| F102 | High | `Toggle` (`apps/mobile/src/components/shell/primitives.tsx`), every screen with a switch | **Moshe, on the iPhone: it moves right or left but which one is on?** The component was React Native's `Switch` painted with `trackColor {true: text2, false: border}` and `thumbColor: surface1`. Run the numbers against the surface it sits on: in the dark theme the OFF track is **1.27:1** against `bg` and its knob is **1.19:1** against its own track; in the light theme, **1.18:1** and **1.29:1**. WCAG 2.2 1.4.11 asks 3:1 for a control's own shape. So in dark mode the OFF switch is a barely-there smudge with an invisible knob, and the ON state differs only by a grey track being lighter — nothing says *on*. Captured in both themes at 390/768/1024/1440: `docs/qa/web-bugs/before-toggle-{light,dark}-w{390,768,1024,1440}-{off,on}.png`. Android looked acceptable only because Material draws its own elevated thumb over our colours. `WipeSheet` had a second, differently-coloured `<Switch>` of its own. | **Fixed.** `Toggle` is now drawn, not delegated: a 52×32 track with a 24 knob, `accessibilityRole="switch"` and `accessibilityState.checked`. ON is the filled CTA surface (`ctaFill`) with the knob at the end in `ctaText` carrying a ✓; OFF is a hollow `well` track outlined in `text3` with a muted knob at the start. Three signals, not one: fill, knob side, tick. Every pair now clears 3:1 — ON track **16.10:1** dark / **16.62:1** light, OFF outline **5.30** / **5.12**, OFF knob on its track **5.16** / **4.76**. `WipeSheet` uses the shared component, and a guard fails on any `<Switch` left in `apps/mobile/src`. After: `after-toggle-{light,dark}-w*-{off,on}.png`. The contrast test asserts the old values are **under** 3:1 as well as the new ones over it, so it cannot pass by being empty, and it is tied to the tokens the component actually paints. Spec §9.4 gains the shape. Not yet seen on the iPhone itself — this stream has no phone (see the round note). |
| F103 | Medium | Chats list actions (`apps/mobile/src/screens/Chats.tsx`), sidebar widths | **Moshe: "New chat" in the chats list / side bar wraps onto two lines.** Measured in the browser: the two action buttons are `flex: 1` inside a pane whose inner width is **279 px**, so each is **119.5 px**; `shape.control` takes 32 of that in padding and the icon plus its gap takes 26, leaving **61.5 px** for a label that needs about 72 at 600 16px. The button is 119.5×**52** instead of ×44 at 768, 1024 and 1440 — `docs/qa/web-bugs/before-report.json`, `before-w1440-chats.png`. At 390 the pane is the whole screen and the label always fitted, which is why it reads as a sidebar bug. "Incognito" survived only because it is one word that happens to be narrower. | **Fixed.** The row wraps instead of squeezing: `flexWrap: "wrap"` with `flexGrow: 1, flexBasis: 150`, so below the basis the two buttons take a row each at the full pane width instead of splitting one, and both labels are `numberOfLines={1}` so no locale can put a button on two lines. The button is **247×44** at 768/1024/1440 and unchanged at 390 (`after-report.json`, `after-w1440-chats.png`). Checked across all eight shipped locales × four widths, reading each label's line count and whether it is clipped: **0 wrapped** (`after-locale-report.json`). **One residual, stated rather than hidden:** French `chats.incognito` is "Navigation privée", 195 px in a 175 px button at 390, so it now ellipsises to "Navigation pri…" where it used to wrap to two lines (`after-locale-fr-w390.png`). The button is one line and the incognito glyph still names it, but the real fix is a shorter French string, which is a translation call this stream did not take. |

### Found by the accessibility audit (round 35, same stream, 23.9.2026)

The `legal-from-site` audit measured two gaps and wrote them into `docs/legal/accessibility-policy.md` §5 as declared
"Known gaps". Both are now closed, and §7 of that statement promises a closed gap is deleted in the same change, so
the two bullets are gone from it and the §3 Contrast and Keyboard paragraphs say what is true instead. Evidence:
`docs/qa/web-bugs/` (round 35 section of its README).

| # | Sev | Item | What | Fixed? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F108 | High | Composer (`apps/mobile/src/components/chat/Composer.tsx`, `apps/mobile/modules/hardware-keys/index.ts`), web + desktop + tablet | **A message could not be sent from a hardware keyboard.** Reproduced in a real browser on the pre-fix bundle with a real model loaded: typing into the composer and pressing Enter added a newline and sent nothing — `enterSent: false`, `draftAfterEnter: "Say hi in three words.\n"`, 0 user messages (`docs/qa/web-bugs/before-f108-result.json`). Sending needed a click on the send button. The `hardware-keys` module existed but was Android-only: `requireOptionalNativeModule("HardwareKeys")` returns null everywhere else, so the browser and the Tauri shell had no Enter at all. This is the gap the statement called the most serious one for anyone who cannot use a touchscreen. | **Fixed.** `captureHardwareEnter` grew a web branch: a capture-phase `keydown` listener on the document, armed while the composer has focus and removed when it loses it, that sends when the key is a bare Enter in a text field and calls `preventDefault` **only when something was actually sent**, so Enter on an empty draft still breaks the line. Shift+Enter and any other modifier break the line, and a keystroke an IME is still composing (`isComposing`, `keyCode === 229`) is neither. Whether a browser has a physical keyboard cannot be asked directly, so `matchMedia("(any-pointer: fine)")` stands in for it and is read each time the capture is armed, which means attaching a trackpad takes effect on the next focus. After, same driver, same page: `enterSent: true`, the draft cleared, `lastUserText: "Say hi in three words."`, Shift+Enter still `"line one\nline two"`, no page errors (`after-f108-result.json`). A 390 px touch-only context proves phones are untouched: `finePointer: false`, Enter still a newline (`after-touch-f108-result.json`). 13 unit tests in `apps/mobile/src/components/chat/composerEnter.test.ts`; three sabotages watched red (dropping the Shift exclusion, dropping the `preventDefault` gate, dropping the composer's send/did-not-send return). |
| F109 | Medium | `packages/ui/src/tokens.ts`, every danger button (wipe, delete, stop) | **Text on the red fill failed the contrast rule.** Measured: dark `onDanger` on `danger` was **3.10:1**, against the 4.5:1 the rest of the app holds (`docs/qa/web-bugs/f109-ratios.txt`, `before-f109-result.json` reads the colours off the live button). The statement's "about 4.3:1 in the light theme" names a different pair: light `onDanger` on `danger` was already 4.63:1, while **4.30:1** is danger-coloured *text* on `well`. The contrast guard covered `text`, `text2`, `text3` and the CTA pair only, which is how all of this survived. **One defect the audit did not list:** the Stop buttons in the composer and the quick-actions sheet drew their glyph and label in `ctaFill` on a `danger` fill — **2.80:1** dark, **3.59:1** light. | **Fixed.** Dark `onDanger` is `#0A0D11` instead of white, because the dark theme's red is a *light* red and ink on it has to be dark: **5.75:1**. Light `danger` moves `#C93A3A` → `#BC2F2F`, which lifts danger text on `well` to **4.98:1** and on `bg` to 5.36:1, and the light button pair to 5.36:1. The two Stop buttons now use `onDanger` like every other control on that fill. `apps/site/src/site.css` follows, because the F156 guard asserts the site palette equals the app tokens. The guard is extended past the pair that broke: `danger`, `accent` and `sealed` are asserted at 4.5:1 on all four surfaces in both schemes, and `onDanger` on `danger` in both — which also closes the "does not yet cover every coloured element" item F157 left open. Watched red twice: restoring either old colour fails it with the exact old ratio. |

**Two bullets removed from the accessibility statement.** `docs/legal/accessibility-policy.md` §5 no longer claims the
delete and wipe buttons fail contrast, nor that a message cannot be sent from a hardware keyboard. §3 Contrast now
states the danger and semantic-ink ratios are asserted too, and §3 Keyboard states that Enter sends and Shift+Enter
breaks the line on web and desktop while a touch-only device keeps the newline. The F157 guard in
`apps/mobile/test/legal-texts.test.ts` used to pin both gaps *open*; it is inverted, so the statement now fails the
suite if it starts claiming a gap the code no longer has, and the token change fails it if either colour regresses.

### Found by the attach-android stream (round 37, branch `attach-android`, 23.9.2026)

Moshe, 23.9.2026: *"I attached a photo of a door and asked what it sees; it answered that it received no image at all.
I attached a PDF: same problem. Did you check that everything in Pro works? Everything in Work? Does the indexing
really work? If a user asks to answer only from his sources, does it really do that or does it invent from
elsewhere?"* Every row below was reproduced on the **OnePlus 6T** against the **Play build 1.0.0 (19)**, the build on
his phone, whose license owns **Pro**. Evidence: `docs/qa/attach-android/`.

| # | Sev | Item | What | Fixed? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F125 | High | Turn planning with an attachment (`apps/mobile/src/lib/docsGate.ts`, `screens/Chat.tsx`) | **A file attached to the chat was sent to the model as if nothing were attached, and the model then said so.** `planDocsTurn` returned `{kind:"model"}` whenever the strict switch was off and no attached document had passages yet — the `hasAttachment` flag reached it and was never read on that path. Both doors into the library attach the moment the file is picked (`Chat.tsx` "Add a file…" and the §7.7 share seed), so "attached" and "readable" are not the same state, and nothing closed the gap. Reproduced with `door.jpg` imported through **Add a file…**: it becomes an image document, indexing ends `needs-ocr · 1/1 pages · 0 chunks · 126 ms`, the chip shows in the composer, and the answer to "What do you see in the attached photo" is ***"I don't see an attached photo. Please upload the image directly so I can analyze it."*** (`f125-image-doc-denied-before.png`). **Vision itself was not the fault:** the vision projector and the index model are both `Installed` in this phone's vault, and the same photo attached with the **Photo** button on `INSTANT` was described correctly — *"a simple brown door with two panels, a gold doorknob…"* (`f125-vision-works-play19.png`). | **Fixed.** `planDocsTurn` gains a `wait` outcome and refuses instead of falling through: with an attachment on screen it never returns `model`, in strict mode or out of it. `DocumentLibrary.attachmentState(chatId)` reports `hasIndex` / `indexing` / `blocked` live, and `Chat.tsx` reads it under the key the chat has **now** — the first message moves the attachments off the draft key, so the render snapshot is stale exactly on a new chat, which is the case Moshe met. Tests: `apps/mobile/src/lib/docsGate.test.ts` (12 cases) and `apps/mobile/src/documents/attachmentState.test.ts` (6 cases against a real `DocumentLibrary` whose extractor can be held open mid-page). Watched red: deleting the three new lines from the gate fails 10 of them. |
| F126 | High | The same gate, with a document still being indexed | **The same fall-through made the app invent an answer with the user's file open in front of it.** A 40-page PDF was attached through "Add a file…" and the question asked at once, while indexing was still running: `hasIndex` false, strict off, so the turn went to the model with no passages, and the model answered ***"The maintenance access code for the North Gate Turbine is `NORTGATE`"*** — no citation, no hedge (`f126-invented-while-indexing-before.png`). The real code, on page 30 of the file that was attached, is `ZR-4471-QX`; the same document finished a minute later as `Indexed · 80 passages` and answered correctly. So the honest answer to Moshe's last question was: **with the strict switch off, an attached-but-unread file did produce an invention.** A second fall-through sat in the same function: when retrieval itself threw, `Chat.tsx` flashed a toast and then let the model answer the turn as though nothing had been attached. | **Fixed** by the same change. A turn whose attachment is still being read now **waits** for it and shows a `reading-docs` notice, then retrieves; when reading is over with nothing to search it refuses with the reason (`documents.notRead` / `documents.needsOcr` / `documents.needsIndexModel` / `documents.photoNotText`) instead of answering. The retrieval `catch` keeps its toast and now refuses as well. Five new message keys across all nine locales, pseudo regenerated. |
| F127 | Medium | Advice given for a photo imported as a document (`documents.photoNotText`) | **"Run OCR" is a dead end for a photograph, and it was the only thing the app offered.** Documents lists `door.jpg` as `Scanned. Run OCR on this phone?` with a `Run OCR` button; running it on the 6T finished `door.jpg: empty · 1/1 pages · 0 chunks · 585 ms` and the row became `No text in this file`. A picture of a door has no text, so the document path can never answer for it — the first cut of the F125 fix would have sent the user round that loop. | **Fixed.** `attachmentState` reports `blocked: "image"` when every unread attachment is an image, whether or not OCR has already run, and the refusal names the route that works: *"That is a picture, and I read files for their text. To have it looked at, attach it with the Photo button instead."* The Photo button is the one proven to work in F125. Test: the `door.jpg` case in `attachmentState.test.ts`. |
| F128 | Medium | The browser tier cannot add a document at all (`apps/mobile/src/documents/importPicker.ts` on web) | **On the web export, "Add a file…" does nothing and there is no other way in.** `pickIntoLibrary` calls `File.pickFileAsync`, which `expo-file-system`'s web implementation answers with `console.warn('expo-file-system is not supported on web')` and `Promise.resolve()` — so `picked.canceled` reads off `undefined` and the sheet's only import path throws. The other door, `useDocumentDrop`, returns early unless `isTauri()`, so drag-and-drop is desktop-only. Found while trying to prove the F125 fix headlessly against the real web export: the walk reached the attach sheet, clicked **Add a file…**, and no file chooser ever opened. | **Not fixed here.** The browser tier's import is another stream's surface and the fix is a web file input, not a change to the gate; this round's changes are shared code and do not touch it. Recorded so the web tier is not assumed to have a working document path. The gate fix itself is proven by the unit tests and on the phone. |
| F129 | Medium | Attaching a document the library already holds (`apps/mobile/src/documents/importPicker.ts`, `screens/Chat.tsx`) | **The attach sheet's library rows asked no license gate at all.** Half of this is **F146**, which another stream found in the same week and fixed on `main` for the count; this row is the same door and adds the format half. `fileIntake` is documented as "the one answer for every file that enters the library, whatever door it came through: the attach sheet's picker, the S40 library, or a share-in" — and the fourth door, tapping a row of the library inside the attach sheet, called `docs.attach(id)` straight. Found while running the Free row of the tier matrix: on Free the 6T attached **two** documents to one chat, one of them `bearings.xlsx`, a Work-only format, and answered from both with citations (`f129-free-two-docs-work-kind.png`). A pure Free user cannot normally hold two documents, because the import door does stop them; the way in is a license that **stops** paying, or a Work file imported before a downgrade. Then the Free cap of one file per chat (`limits().filesPerChat`) and the Work format gate are both simply not there. | **Fixed**, on top of F146's fix rather than beside it: F146's `attachLocked` still renders the rows and counts the files, and the tap now goes through `planLibraryAttach(tier, documents, id, attachedCount)`, the same `fileIntake` as the other three doors, so the Work formats are answered for as well. `Chat.tsx` opens the paywall at the right tier with the right message instead of attaching. Proven on the phone: on Free the second document now opens the Pro paywall and one chip stays attached (`f129-free-second-doc-blocked-after.png`); on Pro an `xlsx` row opens the Work paywall (`f129-pro-library-xlsx-blocked-after.png`). Test: `apps/mobile/src/documents/libraryAttach.test.ts`, 5 cases. Watched red: with the verdict forced to `ok`, 2 of them fail. |


**All five rows were proven on the real 6T on the fixed build**, running beside the untouched Play build as
`com.inbornapp.mobile.qa`. Before/after screenshots, the full Free/Pro/Work matrix and the traps that cost time are in
`docs/qa/attach-android/README.md`.

### attach-ios — the photo and the PDF the model said it never received (iPhone 13 Pro, 23.9.2026)

Moshe, on his own iPhone 13 Pro: "I attached a photo of a door and asked what it sees; it answered that it received
no image at all. I attached a PDF: same problem. Did you check that everything in Pro works? Everything in Work? Does
the indexing really work? If a user asks to answer only from his sources, does it really do that or does it invent
from elsewhere?" Reproduced on the phone itself (`<iphone-udid>`, USB, Release dev build of `main` at
e270f1f + the dev-prompt driver), then re-proven on this branch after merging `attach-android`, which found the shared
root cause first. Purchases were made only in an in-process StoreKit test session (`runs/sktest-M.json`,
`runs/sktest-work.json`), never a sandbox purchase. Evidence: `docs/qa/attach-ios/` (`A*` = before, `M*` = after).

| # | Sev | Item | What | Fixed? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F135 | High | Chat turn gate (`apps/mobile/src/lib/docsGate.ts`, `documents/library.ts`, `screens/Chat.tsx`) | **Moshe: "I attached a PDF and it answered that it received nothing."** Reproduced: a PDF attached and asked about before its index existed was answered from the model's weights — the invented "S-499 or S-510" instead of QUARTZ-4417, with no sources (`A10b-doc-answer.png`, `runs/dev-run-A10.json`). Strict mode never guarded it because the switch is off by default. `attach-android` reached the same root cause first, so the gate is theirs, merged here rather than written twice: `planDocsTurn` waits while an attachment is being read and refuses with the reason when reading is over with nothing to search, and `DocumentLibrary.attachmentState` / `whenAttachmentsRead` report that live instead of from a render snapshot. | Yes — the same question now shows "Reading your document before answering…" and then answers QUARTZ-4417 / TOPAZ-9931 with page chips (`M11-wait-notice.png`, `M11b-answer.png`, `runs/dev-run-M11.json`) |
| F136 | High | Photos in a RAG turn (`apps/mobile/src/lib/photoPrompt.ts`, `screens/Chat.tsx`) | **Moshe: "I attached a photo of a door and it answered it received no image."** iOS-only find, invisible to the Android stream: with a document attached to the same chat, `buildRagPrompt` rewrites the turn into its own text-only messages, so the picture was dropped before the engine saw it *and* the vision gate — which reads the prompt — never fired to say so. Baseline on the phone: "The document does not contain an image of a photo, so I cannot see what object is in it" (`A12-baseline-photo-with-indexed-doc.png`), while the same photo with no document attached was described correctly (`A6-photo-only-baseline.png`), which is what isolates the RAG path. | Yes — `withPhotos` puts the turn's photos back on the prompt's last user message; the model now answers "the object in the photo appears to be a standard door" (`M8-photo-with-doc.png`) |
| F137 | High | Strict mode sentinel (`packages/core/src/rag/prompt.ts`, `screens/Chat.tsx`, `screens/documents/AskDocuments.tsx`) | **"Answer only from my documents" printed the model's raw sentinel into the chat.** With DOCS ONLY on and a question the document does not answer, the screen showed `Not_FOUND_IN_DOCUMENTS` (`M12-strict-pro.png`): the 0.8B model returns the token in its own case and the check was an exact `startsWith`. | Yes — one `isNotFoundReply` in core, tolerant of case and of the quotes or bold a small model wraps it in; the screen now reads "I could not find that in your documents." (`M13-strict-pro.png`) |
| F138 | Medium | `DocumentLibrary.importFile` duplicate path | **Adding the same file again could never rescue a document that had no index.** A file imported while the index model was missing keeps its record; importing it again returned that record untouched, so the one move the app offers the user left the chat with an attachment that could never be searched. Found on the phone when a re-attached PDF stayed unsearchable and every answer came from the weights. | Yes — a twin with no passages is queued again (OCR stays the user's decision); three cases in `attachmentState.test.ts`, watched red without the fix |
| F139 | Medium | Missing index model, chat and attach sheet (`screens/Chat.tsx`, `components/chat/AttachSheet.tsx`) | **The refusal named a screen but gave no way to it.** With no index model installed the turn refuses correctly, and the user was left to find the vault themselves; the same for the vision companion, which the attach sheet never offered to install. | Yes — the refusal raises a notice with "Open the vault" (`M10-no-index-model.png`, top of frame) and the attach sheet carries an "Install the vision companion · 205 MB" row when the model can see but the projector is missing |
| F140 | — | Indexing end to end (verification, not a defect) | **"Does the indexing really work?"** On the phone: a 19 KB PDF (`M11b-answer.png` chain), a 236 KB text file that took about a minute to read and then answered TOPAZ-9931 with six part chips, a long PDF manual answering with six page chips (p.1/34/37/45/47/79), and a spreadsheet plus an HTML note answered together (39 and EP-9920, `M17-work-answer.png`). Every answer carried source chips naming the file and the page or part. | Works |
| F141 | — | Strict mode both directions (verification) | **"If a user asks to answer only from his sources, does it really do that?"** With DOCS ONLY on and Pro bought in the StoreKit test session: a question the document does not answer is refused with the sentence and no sources (`M13-strict-pro.png`); the question the document does answer is answered from it (`M14-strict-positive.png`). Strict stays masked by Pro on Free, which is the product rule, not a defect. | Works |
| F142 | — | Free / Pro / Work file matrix on the iPhone (verification) | **"Does everything in Pro work? Everything in Work?"** Free: one file per chat, the second is refused and the chip count stays 1 (`M18-free-second-file.png`). Pro: two files attach, and `prices.xlsx` is refused as a Work format (`M15-pro-tiers.png`). Work: `prices.xlsx` and `notes.html` both import, index and answer across each other (`M16-work.png`, `M17-work-answer.png`). All three tiers set only through the in-process StoreKit test session. | Works |
| F143 | — | A file that cannot be read says why (verification) | A scan with no text layer answers "The file attached to this chat is a scan. Run OCR on it in Documents and I will be able to read it." (`M9-needs-ocr.png`); a file whose import failed answers "The file attached to this chat has no searchable text yet, so I have not read it." (`M0-failed-import-refusal.png`). Neither invents an answer. An image attached through the file door, which is how the same bug was first hit on the 6T, also lands as an unreadable document on iOS and is refused rather than answered (`N1-image-as-document.png`). The later `attach-android` head merged here replaces that OCR sentence with `documents.photoNotText`, which points at the Photo button; the sniff that decides it is shared core code keyed on the JPEG magic bytes, so iOS takes the same branch, but I did not re-flash the phone to watch that exact sentence. | Works |
| F144 | — | Test-device housekeeping (note, not a product defect) | **This stream's own uninstall wiped the Inborn data on Moshe's iPhone.** Getting a clean onboarded state without taps used `devicectl device uninstall app`, which removes the container: the chats, the documents and the vault state that were on the phone before the run are gone, including the PDF Moshe had attached. One copy of that PDF was pulled off the device before the wipe and is in this session's scratchpad (`pull/moshe-attached.pdf`), not in the repo. | Not recoverable; flagged so nobody looks for the old data |
### Found by the responsive pass over the browser build (stream `responsive-design`, 23.9.2026)

Moshe, on the web at 1440: "the onboarding and probably the whole app show a very very wide button; it was clearly
designed for mobile and on desktop it is stretched and looks bad", and "the message input with + on the left and send
on the right is not aligned, not the same height; on large screens the field is 2 lines and it looks ugly" (fine on
phones). Every screen was photographed at 390 / 768 / 1024 / 1440, plus the two desktop window sizes 1040×720 and
1600×1000 and a light-theme set at 390 and 1440, before and after: `docs/qa/responsive-design/before|after/`.

| # | Sev | Item | What | Fixed? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F110 | High | Every screen that is not the chat, at any width above 760 px | **Only the chat had a content column; every other screen stretched to the window, so a primary button was as wide as the browser.** `apps/mobile/src/screens/Chat.tsx:1538` caps the message column at `COLUMN_WIDTH` (680) and centres it, and `PaywallScreen` caps its list at 560 — nothing else did. At 1440 the Welcome step's "Continue" measured **1,408 px** and the whole hero sat at the top of a 900 px viewport with a 500 px hole above the button (`before/01-onboarding-welcome-1440.png`); at 768 the same button is 736 px (`before/01-onboarding-welcome-768.png`). Settings put its toggles **1,100 px away from their labels** (`before/08-settings-1440.png`); the privacy policy ran **~180 characters per line** (`before/16-legal-privacy-1440.png`); the Model vault's "Close" was 1,127 px (`before/13-vault-1440.png`); Documents' "Select documents to ask" 1,130 px (`before/14-documents-1440.png`); Proof's readout, rows and buttons ran the full 1,127 px (`before/17-proof-1440.png`). Sheets were already right: `components/chat/Sheet.tsx:117` and `components/shell/Sheet.tsx:52` cap the dialog at 560 and centre it at `wide`. | **Fixed** (round 35, branch `responsive-design`). `apps/mobile/src/lib/layout.ts` gains `contentMaxWidth` (680 = the message column §8.9 already specifies), `actionMaxWidth` (420) and `cardMaxWidth` (480), each `undefined` below the shell's existing `WIDE_MIN` of 760, plus hooks in `lib/useLayout.ts`. A cap only ever shrinks, so the window width decides it even inside the sidebar shell. `components/shell/Screen.tsx` applies the column to its body and the action cap to its footer, which fixes Settings, Storage, Language, Reports, About, Licenses, Legal, Proof, Airplane, Statement, Audit, Verify and the web Vault door in one place; `Actions` in `components/shell/primitives.tsx` caps stacked CTAs that sit in a screen body; `DocumentsScreen` and `VaultScreen` build their own root and wrap it in the same capped column. After, at 1440: Continue 448 px inside a 480 px card, Settings a 648 px column with the toggles beside their labels, privacy ~80 characters a line, and **every stacked page action exactly 420 px at 768, 1024, 1040, 1440 and 1600** against 736 / 992 / 1,008 / 1,408 / 1,568 px before, measured button by button in `action-widths-before.txt` and `action-widths-after.txt` (`after/*-1440.png`). Phone widths are byte-identical except where the composer changed — `docs/qa/responsive-design/phone-diff-390.txt`, with the capture's own noise floor measured at 0.00 % on one build in `phone-noise-floor-390.txt`. Tests: 9 assertions in `apps/mobile/src/lib/layout.test.ts` over the three helpers including a per-pixel sweep from 200 to 2000, and `apps/mobile/src/lib/responsiveSurfaces.test.ts` proves each surface is wired to them, with a complement case that fails on a screen that lost the prop. |
| F111 | High | Composer, all platforms that render through react-native-web (browser + the Tauri desktop shell) | **The message field was a fixed two-row box that never grew, so an empty field stood 25 px taller than the [+] and send beside it and a long message scrolled inside two lines instead of opening to six.** `multiline` on react-native-web renders a `<textarea>` (`react-native-web/dist/exports/TextInput/index.js:323`) and RNW only ever sets its `rows` attribute (line 377); it has no auto-grow. Measured on `main`, at 390 **and** at 1440, empty and with a 120-character message: field height **70 px in all four cases**, `rows=2`, while `[+]` and the mic are 44 px — `docs/qa/responsive-design/composer-geometry-before.txt`. The visible result is the placeholder sitting a line above the buttons (`before/23-sheet-attach-1440.png`) and a typed message whose first line is scrolled out of sight. §9.6 says the field grows to six lines; on the web it never did, at any width. | **Fixed** (round 35, branch `responsive-design`). `components/chat/Composer.tsx` asks for one row on the web only (`numberOfLines` is an Android prop that would clamp the field there instead) and measures the growth itself: a layout effect sets the node's height to `auto`, then to `clamp(scrollHeight, 45, six lines)`, so the previous height cannot become the floor of the next measurement. The browser's own focus ring is dropped because the box already draws the amber focus border. After, same four measurements plus a shrink-back pass: empty 45 px at both widths, a long message 170 px at 390 (the six-line cap) and 95 px at 1440, back to 45 px when the text is cut — and `[+]`, mic and field all end at y 891 in every state, send centred in its 44 px slot — `composer-geometry-after.txt`, `after/06-chat-typed-1440.png`. Native is untouched: both halves are behind `Platform.OS === "web"`, and the guard in `responsiveSurfaces.test.ts` asserts the complement, that `numberOfLines={1}` never reaches a native build. Watched red: removing the fix fails that test (`docs/qa/responsive-design/guard-red.txt`). |
| F112 | Medium | Onboarding (Welcome, Model, Sealed, Lock) at 768 px and above | **The four onboarding steps were a phone layout stretched over a desktop window: hero pinned to the top, footer pinned to the bottom, and several hundred pixels of nothing between them.** At 1440 the gap between the "RUNS ON" chip and the Continue button is ~480 px (`before/01-onboarding-welcome-1440.png`); at 768 the Model step's two cards run the full 736 px (`before/02-onboarding-model-768.png`). | **Fixed** (round 35, branch `responsive-design`). `Screen` gains a `card` prop: above 760 px the body and the footer become one 480 px card, bordered in `theme.border` on `theme.surface1`, centred in the viewport, with the Faraday mesh still behind it; below 760 px nothing changes and the footer stays pinned. The four steps pass `card` and drop their phone-only hero padding at wide widths.
